- 11 FRAG, 13 HMG, 33 SMOKE GRENADE, 34 SPEARHEAD, 38 WRENCH, 50 Zombie Bite, 52 Zombie Scrape, 53 Zombie Swipe, 69 RPG-7V [TBG-7V], 101 M1878 Sawed Off Slug, 102 M1878 Shotgun Buck, 103 M1878 Shotgun Buck Gear, 104 M1878 Shotgun Slug, 105 M1878 Shotgun Slug Gear, 125 Helo Signal Smoke (Support), 126 Helo Signal Smoke (Transport), 141 Minigun, 151 K413, 227 Shield Turret

### For 248: performance findings (5 Haiku agents, 2026-10-16)

- [ ] [major, lens] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:203: Worker and band threads run at AboveNormal, above the main thread.
  What: `_thread = new Thread(WorkerLoop) { ..., Priority = ThreadPriority.AboveNormal }` (line 203), and the three band threads at line 209 with the same priority. Every frame with a flash, hit, low health, glow or drops, the worker wakes and the four threads run RenderBand over the whole picture (2 eyes x 129,200 px, about 258k pixels). The Unity main thread runs at Normal priority. The constructor comment (201-202) records why AboveNormal was chosen: 30-80 ms workers came after the flash.
  Cost: Rough estimate, not measured: several ms of CPU per flash frame summed over the four threads (tail, starburst and tone passes at about 10-25 ns per pixel under Mono). When no core is idle, a ready AboveNormal thread preempts a Normal main thread, so each such frame can cost the main thread up to the time these threads hold a core. The size of this effect depends on core count, which is not known here.
  Change: Run the band threads at ThreadPriority.Normal (or BelowNormal), and possibly the worker too. Measure main-thread frame time and LensEffects.WorkMs/WorkMsMax before and after. If pictures then arrive too late, keep only the worker AboveNormal, or size Bands from Environment.ProcessorCount (for example count minus 2).
  Risk: Pictures arrive later under load, so a short flash may be dropped or fade weakly (the flash lives 60 ms). This is a latency trade-off the original author chose deliberately. No change to output.
- [ ] [major, held] src/Weapons/VRScope.cs:283: Scope lens renders the whole scene a second time each frame while raised to the eye.
  What: BeforeRender (VRScope.cs:243-285) runs every frame from HeldWeapon.BeforeRender (HeldWeapon.cs:1179-1187). When `aiming || dist < 0.22f` (VRScope.cs:252) and the eye is within about 7 cm of the sight line, `_camera.enabled = brightness > 0.004f;` (line 283) renders a full second picture. `_camera.cullingMask = vr.cullingMask & ~IgnoreLayers;` (line 264) draws the whole world into a RenderTexture of VRConfig.ScopeResolution (768, VRConfig.cs:247-248) with antiAliasing = 2 (VRScope.cs:77). Frequency: every frame the lens is live. The headset camera is multi-pass stereo (the comment at VRRig.cs:967-968 says so), so the world is already submitted twice per frame and the lens adds a third submission. The frame is CPU-bound (GPU about 4 ms), so the cost is culling and draw submission, not pixels. Six camera properties are also rewritten every frame (VRScope.cs:259-270).
  Cost: Estimate, not measured: one extra culling and draw submission of the scene while the lens is live, about +50% of the world's draw-submission CPU, plausibly 1 to 4 ms per lens frame. Lowering ScopeResolution, as the earlier review suggested, cuts only GPU fill and does not shorten a CPU-bound frame.
  Change: Option A: render the lens on alternate frames by changing line 283 to `_camera.enabled = brightness > 0.004f && (Time.frameCount & 1) == 0;`; the lens material keeps the last picture. Option B: set `_camera.depthTextureMode = DepthTextureMode.None;` once in the VRScope constructor (near VRScope.cs:90-94) instead of copying it from the VR camera at line 269, if the reference camera's value is not None (VRRig.cs:969 copies it into the headset camera). The lens material (Sprites/Default, VRScope.cs:43 and 98) does not read depth. Option C: write lines 259-270 only when the source values change.
  Risk: A: the lens updates at 45 Hz on a 90 Hz headset and may judder while the head or gun moves. B: scene shaders that sample _CameraDepthTexture (soft particles, water) would render differently inside the lens. C: none. Check A and B in the headset before keeping them.
- [ ] [minor, hands] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1735: Cupped pistol's support hand re-solves all fingers every frame.
  What: SnapHands (called every frame from HeldWeapon.BeforeRender, line 1166) sets `off.Model.SolidVersion = Time.frameCount; // the gun hand's fingers move: no reusing last frame's solve` while a Foregrip hand cups a pistol. GripMoved (HandModel.cs:914-933) compares `_slideAtVersion != SolidVersion`, so it is true every frame. That disables the reuse test (HandModel.cs:690-692 requires !moved), so all five SolveFinger calls (HandModel.cs:699-701) run each frame, SettlePalm calls PalmOffset each frame (HandModel.cs:823-825), and every solve query goes through the support delegate (HeldWeapon.cs:1733), which calls NearSkin.
  Cost: About 1,000 support-solid queries per frame (5 fingers x roughly 20 FirstTouch x up to 9 queries, plus the palm scan), each about 0.15-0.25 us because NearSkin runs its capsule loop and InverseTransformPoint, plus about 100 Chain calls. Roughly 0.15-0.3 ms per frame while the gun is still, against about 0.02 ms when reused. Estimated, not profiled. Frequency: every frame while Foregrip + IsPistol + a solid is set.
  Change: Replace frameCount with a key describing the gun hand's skin as seen from the gun body: give HandModel an internal method that hashes its root pose relative to Rig.GunBody plus its 15 _joint values, each quantized (about 0.5 mm, 0.5 degrees, 0.005 curl), and have the support hand set SolidVersion to that key. Keep GripMoved's relative check unchanged. Use the gun-body frame, not world space: a world-space key changes whenever the gun swings and would re-solve through the whole swing. The gun hand's Apply must run before the support hand's in the same frame for the key to be current (an earlier review cites VRManager.cs:201-208; not re-verified here); if not, it is one frame stale.
  Risk: A quantized key can keep a solve up to one quantum stale, the same class of tolerance GripMoved already accepts (3 mm, 2 degrees). An exact-equality key gives no saving because tracking jitter changes the gun hand every frame. Validate with grip_check.py and the autotest support-hand clipping checks (AutoTest.cs around 2069-2123).
- [ ] [minor, hands] src/Rig/HandModel.cs:793: NearSkin and Chain re-read the root matrix and call InverseTransformPoint per sample.
  What: NearSkin (HandModel.cs:777-798) tests the 15 capsules first, then calls `Vector3 l = _root.InverseTransformPoint(p);` (line 793) for every point that misses, and only then tests the palm box (796-797). It is the inner test of the support solid (HeldWeapon.cs:1733) and of the keep-out (ReloadInteractions.cs:1873, 1891). Separately, `Matrix4x4 m = _root.localToWorldMatrix;` is read per call in Chain (line 1052, about 20 per SolveFinger), in PalmTouches (line 937, up to 21 per PalmOffset) and in GloveIn (line 533).
  Cost: Each native transform read is about 50-100 ns and the capsule loop about 75 ns, so NearSkin is about 0.12-0.15 us and one re-solve calls it about 1,000 times: roughly 0.12-0.15 ms per re-solve frame. The matrix reads add about 10-20 us more. This matters only on re-solve frames, so the win shrinks once finding 1 is fixed.
  Change: In UpdateSkin (HandModel.cs:753-771), store `_worldToLocal = _root.worldToLocalMatrix` and `_localToWorld = _root.localToWorldMatrix` once after the root is set in Apply (lines 617 and 644-649), and refresh them at the top of SolveFinger and PalmOffset (SlideOnGrip moves the root before calling them). NearSkin then uses `_worldToLocal.MultiplyPoint3x4(p)` and tests the palm box before the capsule loop. The palm test is an OR with the capsule test, so reordering keeps the result exact. Do NOT add a bounding-box early return before the palm test (as suggested in perf_hands.txt item 3): it would drop palm hits.
  Risk: Low, provided the cached matrices are refreshed whenever the root moves (Apply at lines 617, 644-649; SlideOnGrip at 886-899; PalmOffset caller at 825-830). Check with the autotest hand-clipping metrics.
- [ ] [minor, hands] src/Rig/HandModel.cs:491: Keep-out search restarts from zero every frame while the empty hand is wedged in the gun.
  What: ShortestWayOut (HandModel.cs:491-521) scans 5 mm steps up to 10 cm over 7 directions with no memory of its last answer. KeepEmptyHandOut (ReloadInteractions.cs:1858-1879) calls it every frame while the empty off hand is within 0.7 m of the grip; it is reached from UpdateGraspAnchor (line 1789) inside ReloadInteractions.BeforeRender. ClearanceAlong (HandModel.cs:463-482) has the same shape for a magazine seat.
  Cost: Hand outside the gun: one full GloveIn, about 10 us per frame. Wedged inside the gun with no clear way out within 10 cm: 25 x 7 = 175 GloveIn calls, each leaving at its first blocked sample (1-4 samples at about 0.25 us), so roughly 0.2-0.5 ms per frame. ClearanceAlong is bounded by about 25 short checks and is not worth changing for speed.
  Change: Before the loop, test last frame's answer, which the public field `KeepOut` still holds at call start: `if (!GloveIn(blocked, tracked + KeepOut)) return KeepOut;`, then fall back to the existing search. Only worth doing if the wedged case shows up in a profile.
  Risk: Not behaviour-preserving: the warm start can return a shift that is not the shortest on the 5 mm grid, so the glove may settle a few millimetres differently. Validate against the seating and keep-out checks (ReloadInteractions.cs:2066-2080 and the autotest keep-out metrics).
- [ ] [minor, lens] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:348: Two full-texture SetPixels32 and Apply calls per frame on the main thread.
  What: In Present(): `_tex[e].SetPixels32(_out[e]); _tex[e].Apply(false, false);` for each eye whenever `_busy && _done`, which is nearly every frame effects are on. Each texture is 380 x 340 RGBA32 (about 0.5 MB), so about 1 MB per frame.
  Cost: Estimate only: about 0.3-0.9 ms of main-thread time per frame while effects show. The managed-to-native SetPixels32 copy is perhaps a third of that (0.1-0.3 ms). The Apply upload stays either way.
  Change: Have the worker write into pinned buffers: allocate a pinned GCHandle on each `_out[e]` Color32[] in the constructor and free it in Destroy. Upload with Texture2D.LoadRawTextureData(IntPtr, int) followed by Apply(false, false). Color32 is r,g,b,a bytes, the same layout as RGBA32. The existing _busy/_done handshake already guarantees the worker never writes `_out` while the main thread reads it, so no new synchronization is needed. Confirm the overload exists in the project's Unity 2020.3 API first.
  Risk: Pinning and handle lifetime (free in Destroy); byte count must match exactly. No change to output.
- [ ] [minor, lens] src/Weapons/LensFlash.cs:211: MuzzleFlashInfo.Of (a GetComponentsInChildren walk) runs 5-6 times per shot.
  What: OnShot line 112 calls `MuzzleFlashInfo.Of(weapon)`. BeforeRender line 128 calls it again when the shot is in the same frame. TryFlash line 211 calls FlashSize, which calls `MuzzleFlashInfo.Of(w)` at line 285. Line 216 calls `MuzzleFlashInfo.Of(w).Particles` and then FlashSize again (a second Of). Line 250 calls `.Light` when the view-flash roll passes. Each Of does GetComponent and GetComponentsInChildren<ParticleSystem>(true) on every muzzle, WeaponAnalyzer.ActiveUpTo per child, and several native property reads. OnShot also calls MuzzleFlashInfo.MuzzleSystems (line 111) and GetComponentsInChildren(false) per muzzle (line 117), allocating arrays each time.
  Cost: Estimate of 20-50 us per Of on a typical muzzle tree, so about 0.1-0.3 ms per shot. On automatic fire at 10-15 shots/s that is one spike of that size on each shot frame. Not measured.
  Change: Call Of(weapon) once in OnShot and store it in a `_pendingInfo` field next to `_pendingEveryShot`. Pass it to TryFlash and to FlashSize (add a parameter). Build `_pendingTrees` from the same walk so MuzzleSystems and the GetComponentsInChildren calls are not repeated. Keep ParticlesOut() and TreeCount() at their current times, because they read live particle counts.
  Risk: Low. Of() reads configuration and activity state. If a shot is deferred by a frame, the info is read one frame earlier, which should not change the result.
- [ ] [minor, lens] src/Weapons/LensFlash.cs:188: Visibility samples the gun volume about 940 times per shot, and again every third frame while glowing.
  What: Visibility (lines 145-180) loops 7 rays. Each ray goes through Blocked (182-194), which steps 6 mm (line 185, up to 160 steps) and calls `held.InsideDrawnGun(...)` (line 188). TryFlash calls Visibility once per eye per shot (line 258). UpdateGlow calls it once per eye every third frame while glowing (line 382). For a muzzle about 0.4 m from the eye, that is 7 x 67 x 2, about 940 samples per evaluation. Each sample goes through HeldWeapon.InsideDrawnGun to GunVolume.InsideModel (GunVolume.cs:238), which does `_inv * (world - _pos)` (a quaternion multiply) and a bounds test.
  Cost: Estimate of 50-150 ns per sample, so 50-140 us per evaluation. That is about 50-140 us per shot, and about 30-100 us per frame on average during sustained fire (with a spike every third frame). Not measured.
  Change: Convert the two endpoints of each ray into the gun volume's local frame once. The transform is affine, so the segment stays a straight segment. Then step in local space and call a GunVolume method that takes local points, so the per-sample quaternion multiply is gone. That method would live in GunVolume.cs and HeldWeapon.cs, which are outside this area.
  Risk: Float rounding can differ in the last bits, which could flip one boundary sample and change a flash strength by one step, rarely. Keep the early exit at the first blocked sample.
- [ ] [minor, lens] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:560: Static equal-height band split makes the lower bands the critical path.
  What: The worker runs RenderBand(0) itself and then waits for bands 1-3 (lines 563-575). Each band is 170 rows x 380 px. The flash starburst (radius up to about 170 px, from line 770) and the wash (line 781) are centred on the muzzle's pixel. That pixel is usually below the lens middle, around texture row 216 of 340, so band 1 (and band 3 for the other eye) holds most of the starburst work, and the frame waits for it.
  Cost: Estimate: the slowest band may take 1.3-1.6 times the mean, so about 20-30% of worker wall time per flash frame. This affects only the worker. It does not touch the main thread, but it shortens how long a flash picture takes and so how long the worker competes for cores.
  Change: Split each eye's picture into 12-16 row strips. Let the four threads take strips from a shared counter using Interlocked.Increment. Per-pixel arithmetic and flash order are unchanged, so the output is identical. Replace the per-band `_washX`/`_washY` scratch with per-thread scratch indexed by the thread's slot.
  Risk: More cross-thread counter traffic. Per-thread scratch must be sized for the full width and height. Output identical.
- [ ] [minor, held] src/Weapons/GunVolume.cs:24: Measurement cache of 12 keys thrashes across weapon switches; each rebuild allocates about 11 MB.
  What: `private const int CacheSize = 12;` (line 24) with LRU eviction in Cached (lines 319-352). Keys in play: the weapon (HeldWeapon.cs:1269), "seated:"+key (HeldWeapon.cs:1517), "part:"+key+part name (HeldWeapon.cs:1567), each magazine type (MagazineProp.cs:1127, ShapeKey) and each dropped-weapon type (WeaponDrops.cs:447, "tp:"+name). Once more than 12 keys are cycled round-robin, each switch evicts the entry it needs next and Build reruns (lines 400-565: 10 render passes, a readback, worker decode). Allocations per build: the readback `request.GetData<Color32>().ToArray()` (line 552, about 1.9 MB), the CloseRecesses temporaries `a, b, fa, fb` (lines 716 and 728-729, four arrays per view), Decode `near` and `farSpan` (lines 645-646), and GrowView `n` and `f` (lines 803-804). A side view of a rifle is about 56k texels, so each float array is about 225 KB, above the 85 KB large-object threshold. Frequency: per weapon draw after eviction, not per frame.
  Cost: Estimate: about 5 to 6 MB transient and 5 to 6 MB retained per volume (six float arrays per view). The 12-entry cache already holds about 60 MB. Several MB of large-object churn per switch can trigger full collections. Not measured.
  Change: Remove the transient churn without changing results: copy the readback with `NativeArray<Color32>.CopyTo` into a reusable Color32[] (one per width and height, reused) instead of `.ToArray()` at line 552, and reuse scratch float buffers for a, b, fa, fb in CloseRecesses (a per-job buffer or a small free-list keyed by length). Do not simply raise CacheSize, because each entry costs about 5 to 6 MB.
  Risk: Scratch buffers must not be shared by two worker jobs running at once: use per-job buffers or a lock. The NativeArray is valid only inside the readback callback, where the copy already happens. Retained memory is unchanged.
- [ ] [minor, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1735: Pistol cupped by the off hand: support fingers and palm re-solved every frame.
  What: `off.Model.SolidVersion = Time.frameCount; // the gun hand's fingers move: no reusing last frame's solve` (line 1735), with the support solid `_supportSolid = p => _solid(p) || !LegacySupportSolid && gunHand.NearSkin(p, GunVolume.Grow)` (line 1733). HandModel.GripMoved (HandModel.cs:914-933) treats any change of SolidVersion as a move (line 923), so `moved` is true every frame. The finger reuse path (HandModel.cs:690) is then off, all five SolveFinger calls run (HandModel.cs:955-1025), and SettlePalm recomputes PalmOffset (HandModel.cs:819-829, 935). Every probe that misses the gun also runs NearSkin over the glove's capsule list (HandModel.cs:777-798). Frequency: every frame while a pistol is cupped, i.e. steady two-handed pistol holds.
  Cost: Estimate, not measured: roughly 500 to 1500 probes per frame at about 100 to 200 ns each (NearSkin dominates), plus the finger chains, so about 0.1 to 0.3 ms per frame.
  Change: Version the support solid by the gun glove's skin, not by the frame. This needs a skin version on HandModel (for example `SkinVersion`, bumped in UpdateSkin when any skin point moves more than 0.5 mm from its stored copy). Then here: `off.Model.SolidVersion = (_magSolidShown ? 1 : 0) + 2 * main.Model.SkinVersion;`, keeping `Time.frameCount` only when main.Model is null. A root-only check on the glove is not enough, because a trigger pull curls the fingers without moving the root.
  Risk: A gun-hand finger that moves less than 0.5 mm keeps the previous support solve, a sub-millimetre difference. The change touches HandModel.cs, outside this file.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/BeltStrip.cs:83: Belt strip rebuilds all 32 rounds and re-uploads its mesh every frame.
  What: Place() (BeltStrip.cs:72-107) runs every frame: ReloadInteractions.BeforeRender calls UpdateBeltStrip (ReloadInteractions.cs:1697), which calls _belt.Place (line 946) whenever show is true (belt in hand, hanging, or laid on a gun with no belt model). The loop `for (int i = 0; i < MaxRounds; i++)` (MaxRounds = 32, line 13) computes `_verts[b + k] = rot * _roundVerts[k] + p;` and `_normals[b + k] = rot * _roundNormals[k];` for all 99 vertices of every round (BuildRound: 7 rings x 11 + 22 link vertices), zeroes the unused rounds, then sets `_mesh.vertices`, `_mesh.normals` (3,168 entries each) and calls `RecalculateBounds()` (lines 104-106). `_beltShownLaid` (set at ReloadInteractions.cs:880) is only cleared when the magazine or cover goes (lines 904-920), so a laid belt on a belt-less gun keeps this running every frame. Runs every frame while shown; zero cost when hidden.
  Cost: About 6,300 Quaternion*Vector3 products, two ~38 KB mesh uploads and a 3,168-vertex bounds scan per frame while shown. Estimated 50-100 us per frame (counted, not measured).
  Change: (1) Loop only the drawn rounds, min(count, rounds), and zero only rounds that were drawn last call but are now beyond count (a full belt saves 4 of 32 rounds, more on a short belt). (2) Store the last from/to/barrel/rounds and return early when they are bit-identical (helps a hanging or laid belt while the gun is still). (3) Compute the mesh bounds once in the constructor as a box covering the belt reach (BeltReach 0.32 m plus the 0.06 m slack used at ReloadInteractions.cs:846) with extra margin, instead of RecalculateBounds() each frame.
  Risk: (1) and (2) give identical geometry. (3) changes only frustum-culling bounds; keep the margin generous so the belt is never culled early. A belt in the hand still rebuilds every frame because its end follows the hand, so the gain there is only about 15-40 percent.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/WeaponDrops.cs:750: Nearest() tests every collider of every dropped weapon every frame.
  What: WeaponDrops.Update (line 583) calls `Nearest(hand.Palm.position)` for each tracked hand that may grab. Nearest (lines 736-763) loops all drops and each drop's colliders: `if (c == null || c.bounds.SqrDistance(palm) > bestDist * bestDist) continue;` where Collider.bounds is a native read. Drops are capped at 16 (VRConfig.cs:291), each with up to 12 colliders (FitModel's ShapeFit.Fit maxPieces, line 516). With two hands that is up to 384 native bounds reads and null checks per frame. Runs every frame while at least one drop is on the ground and the player is on foot.
  Cost: About 30 us per frame worst case (16 full drops); 5-10 us with 2-4 drops. Counted estimate, not measured.
  Change: When a drop is created (Spawn, line 143; DropHeld, line 887) store on DroppedWeapon a radius Reach = max over its pieces of (|piece.Bounds.center| + |piece.Bounds.extents|); the pieces are in the drop's root space and the root keeps unit scale (only the model child is scaled, line 156). In Nearest, before the collider loop, skip the drop when `(d.Go.transform.position - palm).sqrMagnitude > (PickupReach + d.Reach) * (PickupReach + d.Reach)`. That is one native read per drop instead of one per collider.
  Risk: Exact: the skip only removes drops whose colliders cannot lie within PickupReach, so the nearest drop found is unchanged. Assumes the root keeps unit scale and the piece bounds are in root space; if either changes, Reach must be recomputed.
- [ ] [minor, rig-ui] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs (called per frame from src/Core/VRManager.cs:237, outside the listed files):348: Lens pictures uploaded to two 380x340 textures on the main thread.
  What: In Present() (line 315), inside `if (_busy && _done)` the code runs `_tex[e].SetPixels32(_out[e]); _tex[e].Apply(false, false);` for e = 0 and 1 (lines 348-349). CW = W+2*MX = 380 and CH = H+2*MY = 340 (line 35), so each buffer is 129,200 Color32 (about 0.5 MB per texture, about 1 MB per upload). It runs on every frame a finished worker picture is ready, which should be most frames while flashes, hits or drops are on screen (inferred from the job pattern, not measured).
  Cost: Estimate 0.1-0.4 ms of main-thread time on each uploading frame: a managed-to-native copy of 2 x 129k pixels plus Apply. Not measured. Zero on frames with no new picture.
  Change: Have the worker write into a persistent NativeArray<Color32> (or pinned buffer) and upload with Texture2D.SetPixelData / LoadRawTextureData, keeping the same format, mipChain=false and Apply flags. That removes the SetPixels32 managed-to-native copy. Optionally skip the upload when the rendered bytes are unchanged.
  Risk: Byte-identical output if the same format and flags are kept. Check the API exists in the project's Unity 2020.3 build (SetPixelData has been available since 2019.3). Upload-path change only; the worker and timing are untouched.
- [ ] [minor, rig-ui] src/UI/WristHud.cs:148: Wrist HUD refits and recolours its TextMesh every frame while shown.
  What: While the wrist display is up (_alpha > 0.01, the glance), BeforeRender runs every frame: `_plate.Fit(); _plate.SetAlpha(_alpha); _text.color = hp > 60f ? ... : ...;` (lines 148-150). Fit() (TextPlate.cs:48-66) reads `_textRenderer.bounds` and sets the text transform's rotation to identity and back twice. `_material.color` is set in SetAlpha. The text string itself is only rebuilt on change, which is already handled.
  Cost: Estimate 0.05-0.2 ms per frame while the glance is held, zero otherwise. The TextMesh colour is baked into the mesh vertices, so each set may trigger a text-mesh update, and reading bounds may force a pending one. Both are unverified.
  Change: Keep the last colour and last alpha in fields and assign _text.color and the plate material colour only when the value differs. Call _plate.Fit() only on frames where the text was just assigned or the display became visible, plus one extra call on the frame after it shows (the existing comment says the size is known only after layout).
  Risk: The extra post-show Fit call covers the one-frame layout lag the existing comment mentions. Compare the exact float colour values, so the output is identical. No change to the text content logic.
- [ ] [minor, rig-ui] src/UI/VRInputModule.cs:154: Empty-hand keep-out search can run up to 141 glove-solid tests per frame.
  What: KeepEmptyHandOut (src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:1858-1879, called every frame from the reload's grasp-anchor path at line 1789) calls `off.Model.ShortestWayOut(_keepOutBlocked, 0.1f, away)` when the empty hand is within 0.7 m of the grip and the free glove is already inside the gun or the dominant glove. ShortestWayOut (src/Rig/HandModel.cs:491-521) then tries 20 steps of 5 mm times 7 directions, and each probe is GloveIn (HandModel.cs:523-541): two `blocked` calls per skin point plus one per palm point.
  Cost: Normal frames: one GloveIn that returns false early, so negligible. Overlap frames: up to 1 + 140 GloveIn calls, each of several solid tests. The per-test cost of the gun's solid is unknown, so this could be a multi-millisecond spike in that state. Not measured.
  Change: No behaviour-preserving cut I can verify from reading. If the Weapons.Update PerfProbe section shows spikes while the empty hand overlaps the gun, add a conservative bounding-box reject in front of _keepOutBlocked (the gun's solid is measured per weapon; grow the box by GunVolume.Grow) so probes far from the gun return without the solid tests.
  Risk: A box that is too tight changes the keep-out result. It must be conservative (grown by the same margin the solid uses). This sits outside the listed files, in Weapons and Rig, so check with the other agents covering those areas.
- [ ] [minor, rig-ui] src/Input/VRInput.cs:555: Haptic pulse builds two strings even though its log line is already written.
  What: On every successful pulse, Haptic runs `Log.InfoOnce("haptic-ok:" + h, $"Vibration on the {h} controller works ({ControllerTypes[(int)h]}).");` (line 555). Both arguments are built before InfoOnce checks its key, so each pulse makes an enum-to-string key concatenation, a boxed enum, an interpolated message string and a lock with a HashSet lookup. Pulses are event-driven: two per shot (HeldWeapon.cs:901 and 905), per reload, magazine, throw or damage event, and up to 20 per second of menu hover ticks (VRInputModule.cs:163).
  Cost: About 4-6 small allocations (roughly 150-250 bytes) and a lock per pulse. Full auto gives about 20 pulses a second, so about 4-5 KB/s. Small next to the session's total garbage.
  Change: Add `static readonly bool[] HapticOkLogged = new bool[2];`. In the success branch use `if (!HapticOkLogged[(int)h]) { HapticOkLogged[(int)h] = true; Log.Info(...); }` with the same message, so it still logs once per hand. Optionally latch the failure branch (line 551) the same way.
  Risk: None. The log output is the same: once per hand, on the first successful pulse.
- [ ] [tiny, hands] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1732: A captured local allocates a closure every frame in the cupped-pistol branch.
  What: `HandModel gunHand = main.Model;` is captured by the support lambda on line 1733: `_supportSolid ?? (_supportSolid = p => _solid(p) || !LegacySupportSolid && gunHand.NearSkin(p, GunVolume.Grow))`. The delegate itself is cached, but the compiler allocates the capture object when the enclosing if-block is entered, so each frame in this branch allocates one small object.
  Cost: About 32 bytes per frame, roughly 3 KB/s while cupping a pistol. Far too small to explain the GC count, but it is free to remove.
  Change: Read the gun hand's model through a field that is set once when the support solid is created (or read `main.Model` through a field), so the lambda captures only `this` and no per-frame object is created.
  Risk: None. The gun hand's HandModel is created once per VRHand (VRHand.cs:67).
- [ ] [tiny, hands] src/Rig/HandModel.cs:884: Grip search runs all 13 probes in one frame, every 0.2 s while the hand moves on the grip.
  What: SlideOnGrip (HandModel.cs:873-900): `foreach (float step in SlideSteps) { _root.position = start + down * step; _root.position += n * PalmOffset(n); SolveFinger(2, 1f); ... }`, armed by `_searchPending |= moved;` (line 876) and throttled to 0.2 s (line 880).
  Cost: 13 PalmOffset calls (each up to about 21 PalmTouches of 12 gun-solid queries) plus 13 SolveFinger calls: about 0.2-0.4 ms in one frame, every 0.2 s while the grip moves. Averages under 0.03 ms per frame.
  Change: Spread the steps across frames (one step per frame, keeping the search state in fields, and applying `_slideGoal` only when the search completes). Alternatively replace PalmOffset's two linear scans (lines 838-856) with a bisection, after checking that PalmTouches is monotone along the normal over -0.04..+0.02 m.
  Risk: Changes timing: the grip lands up to 13 frames after a move, and a partial search must not be applied. Low payoff; do it only if the 0.2 s burst shows up in a profile.
- [ ] [tiny, lens] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:627: Accumulator cleared every frame for every band even when no flash reads it.
  What: RenderBand line 627 does `Array.Clear(acc, y0 * CW * 3, (y1 - y0) * CW * 3)`, which clears about 775 KB per band (3.1 MB per frame), before it knows whether `flash` will be true. acc is read only in the tone loop under `if (flash)` (line 687). On hit-only or low-health-only frames it is never read. WorkerLoop line 550 also allocates a new Stopwatch every worker frame.
  Cost: Estimate: about 0.2-0.4 ms of worker CPU per hit or low-health frame for the memset, and one small allocation per worker frame.
  Change: Before the clear, compute whether any LensFlash call will run: glow > 1e-3, or any flash with Age in [0, Life]. Clear only in that case. Keep one Stopwatch field and call Restart() instead of StartNew().
  Risk: None to output. acc is read only when flash is true.
- [ ] [tiny, lens] src/Weapons/LensFlash.cs:437: Limiter.Shot allocates a closure and delegate on every call.
  What: `_full.RemoveAll(s => t - s.Key >= Window);` captures the parameter `t`, so every call allocates a display class and a delegate. Shot runs once per eye per shot (line 256), so twice per shot. Separately, TryFlash builds its InfoOnce key and message strings eagerly on every skipped shot (lines 223-224 and 241-242), before Log.InfoOnce takes its lock (Log.cs:63-69).
  Cost: About 2 closure and delegate allocations per shot, roughly 100 bytes. Negligible GC pressure, nothing per frame.
  Change: Replace RemoveAll with an in-place backwards loop over `_full` that keeps the order, or compact in place. Output identical. Guard the two InfoOnce calls with a bool set once per weapon name, so the strings are not built on every skipped shot.
  Risk: None.
- [ ] [tiny, lens] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:1208: LensEye looks up _MainTex by name and resends its property block every camera; quad transforms rewritten every frame.
  What: LensEye.OnWillRenderObject (1207-1209) calls `_block.SetTexture("_MainTex", ...)` (a string-to-ID lookup) and `Renderer.SetPropertyBlock(_block)` on every call. It runs for each of the three quads and each rendering camera while shown. Present() (445-447) also writes localPosition, localRotation and localScale for all three quads every frame while shown, although Distance is constant and Ipd rarely changes.
  Cost: Estimate: roughly 10-40 us per frame (about 6-12 native calls per camera plus 9 transform writes).
  Change: Store Shader.PropertyToID("_MainTex") in a static readonly int. Remember the last (show, picture) pair set on each LensEye and skip SetPropertyBlock when it is unchanged. In Present, cache the last ipd and d and skip the three transform writes when they are equal.
  Risk: None, provided the comparison is against what was last set on the renderer. The property block persists on the renderer between calls.
- [ ] [tiny, lens] src/Weapons/MuzzleFireball.cs:152: Per-frame layer write, string-keyed SetColor, and mainTexture write for shown fireballs.
  What: Place (131-157) sets `b.Go.layer` on every frame (152) and calls `b.Material.SetColor("_TintColor", ...)` by name every frame (156). SetGlow (107) writes `_glow.Material.mainTexture` on every glow frame, even when the texture key has not changed. Place also reads `activeSelf` on all 7 balls every frame (134).
  Cost: A few microseconds per frame.
  Change: Cache Shader.PropertyToID("_TintColor"). Set the layer when a ball is assigned in Add or SetGlow, and only when the muzzle's layer differs. Compare the texture key with the one already on the material before writing mainTexture.
  Risk: The layer is then re-read only at assignment. If a muzzle's layer can change during play, keep the per-frame write. Check weapon layer behaviour before dropping it.
- [ ] [tiny, lens] src/Rig/MudSplash.cs:265: Array.IndexOf over the splat pool per spot, and GameContext.InGame re-read every frame.
  What: Dirty loops up to 48 spots and calls `SetUvs(System.Array.IndexOf(_splats, s), s.Variant)` (265). The non-generic IndexOf is O(48) per spot, so up to about 2,300 comparisons per blast, although Free() (282-297) already knows the index. Subscribe() (161-177) is called every frame from Update (101). Its check at line 165 (`manager == null || !Util.GameContext.InGame || ...`) evaluates GameContext.InGame, which calls GameManager.IsSpectating(), plus SceneManager.GetActiveScene() at line 164, every frame whenever ActorManager exists. Update (114-147) also walks all 48 slots every frame even when none is live.
  Cost: IndexOf: about 10-30 us per blast, per explosion rather than per frame. The per-frame Subscribe check: a few microseconds.
  Change: Have Free() return the index and pass it to SetUvs. In Subscribe, test manager and scene first and return early when already subscribed, then test InGame. Behaviour is unchanged, because an already-subscribed same-scene state returns either way. In Update, skip the slot loop when no spot is live and _shown is false.
  Risk: The subscription must still re-attach after a level load. Keep the scene-handle check.
- [ ] [tiny, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1915: Unchanged local values written every frame (handle, hidden magazine parts, spare parts).
  What: `MovePart(Rig.Handle, d.HandleRestLocal, Vector3.zero);` (line 1915) runs every frame while the slide is at rest. `part.localScale = Vector3.one * 1e-4f;` runs every frame for each magazine part while the magazine is out (line 1936, lines 1932-1938), and `t.localScale = Vector3.one * 1e-4f;` runs every frame for spare parts (line 500, HideSpareParts lines 496-502). Each write sets an unchanged value; the hierarchy is already dirty from the root anchor write each frame (line 1163).
  Cost: About a few dozen native setter calls per frame, tens of nanoseconds each: roughly 1 to 3 us. Larger only if a hidden part carries a collider, which would make each scale write update its shape (not verified).
  Change: Write only on change: read first, e.g. `if (t.localScale != tiny) t.localScale = tiny;` at lines 500 and 1936, and `if (Rig.Handle.localPosition != target) ...` around line 1915.
  Risk: Vector3 equality tolerates about 1e-5 units, so drift under 0.01 mm is not re-applied; no visible change. The gain is negligible, so apply it only where these lines are touched anyway.
- [ ] [tiny, reload] src/Weapons/MagazineProp.cs:1481: Per-frame Vector3[] allocation in KeepOffGun, plus repeated HoldPose work per held magazine.
  What: KeepOffGun (lines 1396-1515) runs from FollowHand (line 1373) every frame a magazine is held within 0.7 m of the grip. While the magazine is blocked by the receiver it executes `foreach (Vector3 axis in new[] { aimW, upW, rightW })` (line 1481), a new 68-byte array every frame. Separately, one held magazine evaluates HoldPose 5-6 times per frame: ReloadInteractions.UpdateHeldProp line 1068; HandAlongRail line 1203 (via 1069); LinedUpUnderWell lines 1215-1216 (via UpdateGraspAnchor line 1794); TryMagKick line 1257 (when a magazine is inserted); and FollowHand line 1362. Each call re-runs HandModel.PalmSurfaceFromGrip (a loop over the palm skin) and recomputes SeatPose.
  Cost: 68 bytes per frame while pressed on the receiver (about 4 KB/s). The HoldPose repeats cost about 3-6 us per frame. Counted estimate.
  Change: Replace the array with a static readonly Vector3[3] filled before the loop, or with three explicit calls. Compute HoldPose and SeatPose once per frame per hand: store (frame, hold, seat) on the prop and pass the already-computed pose into HandAlongRail and LinedUpUnderWell through an overload.
  Risk: A static buffer is safe here (single thread, no re-entry). The cache must be keyed on Time.frameCount and the hand. If the palm changes between Update and BeforeRender, results would differ; scripted autotest moves (VRHand.Scripted) are set between yields, so this is fine. Gain is very small: lowest priority.
- [ ] [tiny, reload] src/Weapons/Casings.cs:182: Casings.Cull reads the transform position of every live case every frame.
  What: Cull (lines 172-189) runs every frame from WeaponSystem.cs:108 and on every Spawn. For each live case it evaluates `now - c.Born > Lifetime || c.Go.transform.position.y < -500f`. The second term runs for every case that has not yet expired, so up to 40 transform and position native reads per frame while cases lie around (they last 25 s).
  Cost: About 40 x 2 native reads, roughly 3-6 us per frame. Counted estimate.
  Change: Keep the lifetime test every frame. Check the y < -500 fall-out only when `now >= _nextFallCheck`, then set `_nextFallCheck = now + 0.5f` (a static float).
  Risk: A case that falls below -500 m is removed up to 0.5 s later, which is not visible. No other behaviour change.
- [ ] [tiny, rig-ui] src/Input/VRInput.cs:222: Seventeen unrestricted digital reads per frame duplicate the two hand reads.
  What: Update() loops every digital action (DigitalCount = 17) over three slots (lines 217-244), so 51 GetDigitalActionData calls per frame. Slot 2, the any-hand read, is a separate SteamVR call (restrict = OpenVR.k_ulInvalidInputValueHandle, line 221) for each of the 17 actions.
  Cost: About 17 fewer native SteamVR input calls per frame, roughly 20-60 us (estimate, about 1-3 us per call). Always on, so it is the one per-frame saving in this file.
  Change: Set slot 2 from the two hand slots instead of a third call: state = Held[a,0] || Held[a,1], with the press, release and Unclaimed logic unchanged for slot 2 and the HeldSince stamp taken from the same edge. Keep the per-hand reads as they are.
  Risk: Only equal if every digital action is bound to a hand source. An action bound to the HMD, a gamepad or any unrestricted device would stop reading for the any-hand slot. Check the embedded bindings_*.json files for non-hand digital bindings before changing it.
- [ ] [tiny, rig-ui] src/UI/CanvasManager.cs:156: Full-scene Canvas search every 0.4 s, even in steady gameplay.
  What: Update() calls Scan() when `Time.unscaledTime >= _nextScan` (lines 95-98, interval 0.4 s), and Scan() does `foreach (Canvas c in UnityEngine.Object.FindObjectsOfType<Canvas>())` (line 156). That is 2.5 scene-wide searches per second for the whole session, each allocating a Canvas[].
  Cost: Unmeasured. It scales with the number of loaded Canvas objects. The 0.4 s period gives one hitch about every 36 frames at 90 Hz, but the average is small. Estimate 0.1-1 ms per call, which is an average of 0.05-0.4 ms per frame.
  Change: Keep discovery but scan at 1 Hz while `GameContext.InGame && !GameContext.GameMenuOpen && !LoadingView.Active`. Keep 0.4 s in menus and the ScanNow() path the game uses for menus. Confirm the cost first by wrapping Scan() in a Log.Guard section and reading the -rfvr-perf report.
  Risk: A HUD canvas that appears during play is shown flat (screen-space) for up to 1 s before it is converted. Only do this if the measurement shows the scan matters.
- [ ] [tiny, rig-ui] src/UI/VRSettingsPanel.cs:318: Settings panel formats about 20 strings five times a second while open.
  What: UISystem.Update (src/UI/UISystem.cs:134-138) calls Settings.Refresh() at 5 Hz while the panel is open. Refresh() (VRSettingsPanel.cs:318-324) runs every refresher. Each refresher is `v.text = value()` (lines 238, 251), and value is a closure such as `() => $"{VRConfig.SnapTurnAngle.Value:0}°"` (line 66), so each row formats and allocates a string on every refresh.
  Cost: About 100 small string allocations per second while the panel is open. Menu-time only, nothing in play. Estimate only.
  Change: Store the last string (or the last underlying value) per row, and assign text only when the formatted value differs. Or compare the config value first and format only when it changed.
  Risk: None to behaviour. Each refresh still compares against the current config value, so changes made by another control still show. This file is outside the listed files; it is UI-only.

Results (2026-10-16 evening; helper = Opus helper on helper-248-249, merged in b07604f; lead = tasks-98-113), in the order above:
- 248.1 LensEffects.cs:203: fixed by the helper (156716d): the band threads run at Normal, the worker stays AboveNormal.
- 248.2 VRScope.cs:283: fixed by the helper (156716d): the scope camera's copied settings and FOV are written only when they change; the second scene render itself stays.
- 248.3 / 248.11 HeldWeapon.cs:1735: fixed by the lead (288f492 HandModel.SkinKey, f8ed393 wired): the support hand is solved again only when the gun hand's skin moves against the gun.
- 248.4 HandModel.cs:793 (NearSkin matrices): not done; it only matters on re-solve frames, which 248.3 made rare.
- 248.5 HandModel.cs:491 (keep-out warm start): not done; it changes behaviour, and the finding asks for a profile first.
- 248.6 / 248.14 LensEffects.cs:348: fixed by the helper (2e28ade): a raw SetPixelData copy instead of SetPixels32.
- 248.7 LensFlash.cs:211: fixed by the helper (9e7c2e8): MuzzleFlashInfo.Of is read once per shot.
- 248.8 LensFlash.cs:188: open (lead, GunVolume): an InsideModel that takes gun-local points, so the flash's visibility ray is transformed once.
- 248.9 LensEffects.cs:560: skipped: rebalancing the bands is a concurrency rewrite for a worker-only gain.
- 248.10 GunVolume.cs:24 (cache of 12 keys thrashing): open (lead).
- 248.12 BeltStrip.cs:83: fixed by the helper (715ebaf): nothing is rebuilt while unmoved; the bounds are set once.
- 248.13 WeaponDrops.cs:750: fixed by the helper (2abbb8a): a reach radius per drop is checked before its colliders.
- 248.15 WristHud.cs:148: fixed by the helper (56cf90d): the text is fitted and coloured again only on change.
- 248.16 VRInputModule.cs:154: skipped: the cut belongs in ReloadInteractions.KeepEmptyHandOut, and the finding asks for a profile first.
- 248.17 VRInput.cs:555: fixed by the helper (56cf90d): no strings are built per haptic pulse.
- 248.18 HeldWeapon.cs:1732: fixed by the helper (6684bf0): no closure object per frame.
- 248.19 HandModel.cs:884 (grip search spread over frames): not done; it changes timing for under 0.03 ms a frame.

- 248.21 LensFlash.cs:437: fixed by the helper (9e7c2e8): no closure in the limiter.

### For 249: bug hunt findings (5 Haiku agents, 2026-10-16)

- [ ] [major, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1922: Fire-mode variant's magazine flag drives the parent's magazine geometry.
  What: ApplyPartOverrides: `AmmoState ammo = ActiveAmmo ?? Ammo;` (1922), then `if (Rig.MagazineParts.Length > 0 && ammo != null && ammo.UsesDetachableMag)` (1925), `bool hide = !ammo.MagInserted;` (1929), `RestoreMagazine();` (1943), and the seat block on `ammo.MagInserted` (1946-1948). The geometry is always the parent's: SubWeaponRig (560-572) returns the parent Rig for a fire-mode variant. But ActiveAmmo is the variant's own AmmoState, whose MagInserted defaults to true (AmmoState.cs:18) and whose UsesDetachableMag is true for any SelfLoading action (AmmoState.cs:45). ReloadInteractions.cs:66 (Ammo => _held.ActiveAmmo) means the pull writes to the parent state only.
  Trigger: Rifle with a game-cycled semi/auto variant. The player pulls the magazine with the parent active (parent.MagInserted=false, the magazine hides and becomes a prop in the hand). They tap the game's fire-mode key (HandleFireModePress at WeaponSystem.cs:478 returns false for guns with sub weapons, so the game cycles them). Next frame Update switches ActiveAmmo to the variant, whose MagInserted is still true, so RestoreMagazine and the seat block put the magazine back into the gun while the prop is still in the hand. The variant's FeedReady (AmmoState.cs:56) reads the same flag, so it can keep firing with no magazine in the gun.
  Change: Drive the parent-geometry magazine block (hide/restore and seat, lines 1925-1948) from the parent state `Ammo` and `Ammo.UsesDetachableMag`, since the geometry belongs to Rig. Also make the variant's magazine presence read through to the parent (AmmoState needs a parent link for variants, used by FeedReady and the magazine flag). Per-variant round counts can stay as they are.
- [ ] [major, world] src/Weapons/WeaponDrops.cs:1077: Own rifle loses only-copy protection, deleted 90 s after drop.
  What: ReturnOwnDrops: `if (actor.dead || d.OwnerSlot >= actor.weapons.Length || actor.weapons[d.OwnerSlot] != null) { d.OwnerSlot = -1; // its place is taken: an ordinary drop from now on continue; }`. Keep is never set on this path (only the swap path at line 786 sets `swapped.Keep = true`). Cull (lines 841-844) removes any drop with `expired && !d.Keep`.
  Trigger: Drop your rifle from slot 0 (it becomes an owner drop; slot 0 is empty). Within a few seconds pick up a fallen enemy's rifle lying nearby: PickUp puts it straight into the empty slot 0 with no swap. About 8 s after the drop (ReturnAt) ReturnOwnDrops finds slot 0 occupied, sets OwnerSlot = -1 and leaves Keep false. The rifle is now an ordinary drop, so Cull removes it once Time.time - DropTime > DroppedWeaponLifetime (90 s after you dropped it). It vanishes from the ground and from your loadout for good, although the Cull comment at lines 835-837 says your own weapon is the only copy and is never away.
  Change: At line 1077 set `d.Keep = true;` together with `d.OwnerSlot = -1;` so the reclassified weapon is protected the same way as a swapped-away one. The new six-cap at lines 852-860 then applies to it as well (see the next finding). Alternatively, exempt it from lifetime removal explicitly.
- [ ] [major, world] src/Weapons/WeaponDrops.cs:852: Seventh swap silently deletes the first swapped-away weapon.
  What: Newest commit 303be9d: `for (int i = 0; i < _drops.Count && kept > 6; i++) { if (_drops[i].Keep && _drops[i].OwnerSlot < 0) { Remove(_drops[i]); i--; kept--; } }`. The field doc at lines 26-27 says `The player's own weapon put down in a swap: the only copy of it, never culled.` and `public bool Keep;`. Commit b0a99c2 also says a swapped-away weapon is never culled.
  Trigger: In one fight, swap into a full slot seven times (pick up a fallen enemy's gun while holding one). Each swap leaves your previous gun as a Keep drop (line 786) that never expires. On the seventh swap Cull removes the oldest Keep drop, which is your first gun put down, with no message.
  Change: Either stop culling Keep drops (apply the six-cap only to ordinary drops and accept that Keep drops are bounded by how many swaps happen), or keep the cap and update the doc at lines 26-27 and commit message so the loss is intended. Confidence is medium because 303be9d deliberately added this cap; the conflict is with the stated only-copy invariant.
- [ ] [major, effects-ui] src/UI/CanvasManager.cs:429: HUD and game-camera aspect taken from whichever visible canvas is enumerated last.
  What: float aspect = 16f / 9f; (line 419), then inside the loop over all visible tracked canvases: if (t.Size.x > 1f && t.Size.y > 1f) { aspect = t.Size.x / t.Size.y; } (lines 427-430), and after the loop: float vfov = 2f * Mathf.Atan(Mathf.Tan(hfov * 0.5f * Mathf.Deg2Rad) / aspect) * Mathf.Rad2Deg; player.fpCamera.fieldOfView = vfov; player.fpCamera.aspect = aspect; (lines 457-459).
  Trigger: Any visible converted canvas whose rect is not 16:9 (a small HUD panel, a 4:3 menu) overwrites aspect for that frame. The value that survives is the one from the last visible canvas in _tracked enumeration order. With hfov 72, the game camera's vertical FOV is about 44 deg when the last one is 16:9 and about 57 deg when it is 4:3, so it jumps when a panel shows or hides. The screen-projected markers (objectives, squad orders, hit indicators, computed through fpCamera) then land in the wrong place on the head-locked HUD frames and move when the visible set changes.
  Change: Do not derive aspect from canvases. Set one aspect before the loop (the screen aspect, or the one the HUD frame is built for), use it for fpCamera, and fit each HUD canvas uniformly inside that frame: scale = Mathf.Min(width / t.Size.x, height / t.Size.y), so every HUD frame and the camera share one aspect.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:1137: MovingIn is set and cleared by per-frame speed, so slow seating never pushes and slow pull-outs never release the push.
  What: `if (prop.RailDepth > prop.PrevDepth + 0.001f) { prop.MovingIn = true; } else if (prop.RailDepth < prop.PrevDepth - 0.001f) { prop.MovingIn = false; }` (1137-1144). The only reader is MagazineProp.cs:1324: `float push = PistolSized && !FromSeat && MovingIn && RailLength > 1e-4f ? Mathf.InverseLerp(0.2f, 0.75f, RailDepth / RailLength) : 0f;`. MovingIn is never set or cleared anywhere else (MagazineProp.cs:53 declares it, ReloadInteractions.cs:539 clears it on pull-out).
  Trigger: The threshold is a speed, not a direction: 1 mm per frame is about 9 cm/s at 90 Hz and 7 cm/s at 72 Hz, so the behaviour also depends on frame rate. (a) A pistol magazine pushed in deliberately slowly never sets MovingIn, so the palm never turns into the base-plate push the comment promises; the hand stays in the grip hold while it slides up. (b) A magazine pushed in to about half way, then pulled back out slowly (under the threshold), keeps MovingIn=true. Push then stays at its depth-driven value (full above 75% depth) and the palm stays on the base plate while the magazine comes out. It only lets go on a pull faster than the threshold. The push therefore follows depth alone, and the direction is never tracked.
  Change: Track direction with a dead band instead of per-frame speed. Keep a turning depth on the prop (e.g. `TurnDepth`, set at each reversal). Set MovingIn=true when RailDepth > TurnDepth + 0.003f and MovingIn=false when RailDepth < TurnDepth - 0.003f, updating TurnDepth to the current depth while moving in the current direction. Or drive it from a depth velocity averaged over about 4 frames with a threshold near 0.2 mm/frame. Either way, check the seated and pulled-from-seat paths (PullSeatedMagazine sets MovingIn=false) still hold.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/MagazineProp.cs:244: Cached grip section is never applied to a magazine built before the cache filled, and palmed magazines never measure it.
  What: `_sectionMeasured = true; if (GripSections.TryGetValue(_sectionKey, out Vector2 known) && known.x > 0f) { return; // from its vertices, or measured on an earlier one }` (244-247). Callers: only ShapeHand (1152), which runs for the fist magazine. The palm loop in ShapeHand (1141-1146) calls only EnsureVolume.
  Trigger: For an unreadable-mesh magazine the constructor gets the box values and caches Vector2.zero (GripSection 303-373). If another instance of that type measures its volume first and fills the cache, then any instance built earlier hits this early return and keeps its box-based _sideHalf/_frontHalf/CrossHalf (288-291 never run) for its whole life. A palmed magazine never reaches MeasureSectionFromVolume at all. So the second magazine of a tactical reload keeps box widths: HoldPose (1022, 1031, 1039) and PistolSized/BoxSized (71, 79) classify it from the box, so a pistol magazine can be held as a box. Example: the first tactical reload of a session palms the old magazine; it is later picked off the floor and held with the box width.
  Change: On the early return, apply the cached values instead of discarding them: `if (known.x > 0f) { _frontHalf = Mathf.Clamp(known.x, 0.006f, 0.1f); _sideHalf = Mathf.Clamp(known.y, 0.006f, 0.04f); CrossHalf = Mathf.Clamp(Mathf.Max(known.x, known.y), 0.006f, 0.1f); return; }`. Also run the same apply for palmed magazines in ShapeHand's loop (1141-1146), not just EnsureVolume.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/MagazineProp.cs:288: A held magazine of an unreadable type changes its hand pose the frame its grip volume becomes Ready.
  What: `bool wasBox = BoxSized, wasPistol = PistolSized; _sideHalf = Mathf.Clamp(sideHalf, 0.006f, 0.04f); _frontHalf = Mathf.Clamp(frontHalf, 0.006f, 0.1f); CrossHalf = ...;` (288-291), reached from ShapeHand (1152) every frame the magazine is held. HoldPose reads these at 1022 (PistolSized ? 0.12f : 0.15f), 1031 (`rest = ...PalmSurfaceFromGrip(zHold - _frontHalf, ...) + _sideHalf`) and 1039 (BoxSized ? 0.03f : 0.01f).
  Trigger: GunVolume.Measure is asynchronous: the read-back is decoded on a thread pool and `_ready` is published last (GunVolume.cs about 583-630). So Ready flips a few frames after the grab. On that frame FollowHand (1356-1379) calls ShapeHand, MeasureSectionFromVolume swaps the half-thicknesses, and HoldPose returns a different palm rest (and possibly a different gripAt or box offset) at once. The only blend is AttachBlend (0.09 s), which covers the Attach moment only (1363-1372), and for a pouch magazine (_fresh) there is no blend at all. The magazine therefore pops in the palm a few frames after the grab, the first time each unreadable type is held.
  Change: Do not change the hold inputs while the magazine is held. Either measure the section before the magazine is first shown in the hand, or blend _frontHalf/_sideHalf/CrossHalf over about 0.1 s after a change, and apply the change only on the next Attach if one is pending. Keep PistolSized/BoxSized consistent with whatever HoldPose uses.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:1171: DepthVel is seeded from the raw hand speed with no cap; the 0.6 / 1.6 m/s limit only bounds the displacement term.
  What: Seed: `prop.DepthVel = Mathf.Max(0f, (prop.RailDepth - prop.PrevDepth) / Mathf.Max(Time.unscaledDeltaTime, 1e-3f));` (1171). Spring: `prop.RailDepth = Mathf.SmoothDamp(prop.RailDepth, prop.RailLength, ref prop.DepthVel, rocketIn ? 0.12f : 0.09f, rocketIn ? 1.6f : 0.6f, ...)` (1116-1117). Comment at 1112: 'at most 0.6 m/s'.
  Trigger: Unity's Mathf.SmoothDamp clamps `change` to maxSpeed*smoothTime but carries currentVelocity through unclamped. A fast push past 25% depth seeds the spring at the hand's own speed (a 2 m/s push seeds 2 m/s, not the documented 0.6 m/s), and the magazine seats within a few frames while the hand is still moving it. A one-frame tracking jump of 2-3 cm seeds about 2-3 m/s and seats it the same way. The documented cap is not what the code does for the first frames.
  Change: Clamp the seed to the spring's own limit: `prop.DepthVel = Mathf.Min(seed, rocketIn ? 1.6f : 0.6f)`, or average the per-frame delta over 3 frames before seeding. Correct the comments at 1112-1114 to match.
- [ ] [minor, reload] src/Weapons/MagazineProp.cs:1108: A null volume from a failed first measurement is latched, so the magazine never gets a hand solid.
  What: `if (_volume != null && !_volume.Failed || _volumeTried && _volume == null || Go == null) { return; }` (1108-1110). The retry branch at 1112-1115 only covers a non-null Failed volume.
  Trigger: GunVolume.Cached (GunVolume.cs 340-343) returns null without caching when Build throws or returns null (GunVolume.cs 405, 434 for a degenerate box). EnsureVolume then sets _volumeTried=true with _volume null, and the null is never retried. ShapeHand returns early (1148) for the magazine's whole life, so HeldShapesOf skips it and the fingers and palm are not kept out of it. A transient exception on the first measurement disables this magazine's hand shape permanently.
  Change: Treat a null result like Failed: store `_volumeRetryAt = Time.unscaledTime + 1f` and retry each second instead of latching `_volumeTried` for null (drop the `_volumeTried && _volume == null` clause and let the timed retry handle it).
- [ ] [minor, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:889: Sub-weapon shots are kicked with the parent's class and rig data.
  What: OnShot: `Weapon.Configuration c = (fired != null ? fired : Weapon).configuration;` (874) then `float kick = ShotKick(c, Rig.Data, _weaponClass, out float climbShare);` (889). The doc comment at 871 says a sub weapon 'kicks as itself', and the casing check at 913 already uses ActiveAmmo/ActiveRig, so only the kick ignores the sub.
  Trigger: Rifle with an under-barrel launcher: firing the launcher gets the rifle's floor (0.7, climb share 1.0) instead of the launcher's (1.2, 0.8), and the one-handed multiplier is the rifle's `_oneHandRecoil` (1.9) rather than the launcher's (1.6), about 19% too strong one-handed. A shotgun sub on an AR is not treated as a shotgun (no 1.6 floor). A sub's round on a bolt-action parent gets the parent's boltRifle rule.
  Change: In OnShot take `WeaponRig firedRig = fired != null && fired != Weapon ? SubWeaponRig(fired) : Rig;` and `WeaponClass kind = fired != null && fired != Weapon ? ClassOf(firedRig, fired) : _weaponClass;`, call `ShotKick(c, firedRig.Data, kind, out climbShare)`, and take the one-handed multiplier from `Handling(firedRig.Data, fired, out oneHand, out _, out _)` for that weapon instead of the parent's `_oneHandRecoil`.
- [ ] [minor, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:994: Scope steadying's 'speed' is filter lag divided by frame time, not hand speed.
  What: SteadyThroughScope: `float behind = Quaternion.Angle(_steadyRot, gunRot);` (993), `float speed = behind / Mathf.Max(dt, 1e-4f); // degrees a second the hand is turning away from the steadied aim` (994), `float cutoff = Mathf.Lerp(1.6f, 8f, Mathf.InverseLerp(4f, 60f, speed)) / ...` (996).
  Trigger: For a first-order filter following a steady turn of v deg/s, the lag settles at v/(2*pi*fc), so `speed` = v/(2*pi*fc*dt). Working the feedback through, a slow 3 deg/s drift settles near 2.9 Hz at 90 Hz and near 2.2 Hz at 45 Hz, instead of the 1.6 Hz floor the comment describes ('1 Hz for a tremor at 4x'). The steadying is weaker for slow motion and depends on refresh rate.
  Change: Use the hand's own angular speed: keep the previous raw gun rotation, compute `handSpeed = Quaternion.Angle(prevRaw, gunRot) / dt`, low-pass it (about 2 Hz), and drive the cutoff from that in deg/s.
- [ ] [minor, held] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:135: Failed setup leaves the gun's physics stand-in alive at the world origin.
  What: Constructor: `_gunCollider = GunCollider.Create(rig);` (135) runs before steps that can throw: `Inertia(...)` reads `w.configuration.ammo` for a single-shot action (165, body near 606), and `new ArmIK(system, this)` (190). WeaponSystem.AttachHeld's catch (WeaponSystem.cs:310-316) sets Held=null and marks the weapon failed but never destroys GunCollider.Current. Release() has the same shape: it destroys the stand-in only as its last statement (2153).
  Trigger: A single-shot weapon with a null configuration (GiveMuzzle at 354 anticipates this) or an ArmIK exception. The stand-in GameObject is created at the world origin and only positioned from GunCollider.Update, which is never called again. Its prop-layer boxes stay at (0,0,0) and it is DontDestroyOnLoad, so dropped magazines and weapons bounce off an invisible gun-shaped box until the next successful draw (GunCollider.cs:494). Scene changes do not clear it because Held is null. Separately, any exception earlier in Release() (swallowed by Log.Guard in WeaponSystem.ReleaseHeld) leaves it at the last held pose.
  Change: Create the GunCollider as the last step of the constructor. In AttachHeld's catch, destroy GunCollider.Current when it Holds(rig) (hoist `rig` out of the try). In Release(), move `_gunCollider?.Destroy()` to the top.
- [ ] [minor, hands] src/Weapons/ArmIK.cs:98: Off-arm anchor offset uses the grip point from the previous frame.
  What: ArmIK.Solve (line 98): `Vector3 anchorWant = anchored ? Vector3.ClampMagnitude(off.Model.GraspAnchor.Value - off.Model.GraspPointWorld, 0.05f) : Vector3.zero;` while line 92 uses this frame's `off.PalmCentre`. GraspPointWorld is written only in HandModel.Apply (HandModel.cs:585, comment at 587-589 says it is 'kept live'). Frame order: VRManager.OnBeforeRender (VRManager.cs:235) runs Weapons.BeforeRender, which calls HeldWeapon.BeforeRender (WeaponSystem.cs:264, HeldWeapon.cs:1167-1169 `_armIK.Solve()`), and only then the hand Apply delegates (VRManager.cs:240-246). So the arm reads the grip point from the previous frame's Apply.
  Trigger: The player racks a rifle bolt or charging handle, or pulls a pistol slide, with the off hand. The off hand is anchored (ReloadInteractions.UpdateGraspAnchor, line 1801), ArmIK is active, the glove is suppressed and the weapon's own arm is drawn. While the hand moves, the arm's palm sits about one frame of hand travel off the grip the glove is holding (about 5 mm at 0.5 m/s, about 1 cm at 1 m/s), and the error goes to zero when the hand stops.
  Change: Compute the current grip point for ArmIK from this frame's palm. Move the target math in HandModel.Apply (lines 561-585: `Palm.Multiply(new RigidPose(posOffset, rotOffset))`, then `TransformPoint(graspLocal)`) into a helper such as `internal Vector3 GraspPointNow()` and call it at ArmIK.cs:98. Alternatively run the grip-point part of Apply before _armIK.Solve(). Keep the GraspPointWorld field for the glove's own offset at HandModel.cs:595.
- [ ] [minor, world] src/Weapons/ThrowAndMelee.cs:334: Pin pops out on grip press when the hand is 5.5-7 cm away.
  What: UpdatePin: the grab test at lines 319-320 (`Distance(off.Palm.position, pin) < PinGrabRadius`, PinGrabRadius = 0.07f) sets `_holdingPin = true`, then in the same frame lines 334-336 run `else if (Vector3.Distance(off.Palm.position, pin) > PinPullDistance) { PullPin(off); }` with PinPullDistance = 0.055f.
  Trigger: The off hand reaches a fused grenade's pin and closes the grip while the palm is 5.5-7 cm from the pin centre. The second check runs in that same frame, sees distance > 5.5 cm, and calls PullPin(off), so the pin comes out with no tugging. A grab closer than 5.5 cm needs an actual pull, so the gesture depends on how far away the grip closed.
  Change: Do not run the pull test on the grab frame. Record the grab point when the grip closes (for example `_pinGrabAt = off.Palm.position`) and pull only when `Vector3.Distance(off.Palm.position, _pinGrabAt) > PinPullDistance`.
- [ ] [minor, effects-ui] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:412: Single-pass: shared picture weights hits by the right eye, so left-side hits glow at 55%.
  What: j.HitEye[0] = side > 0f ? 1f - 0.45f * side : 1f; j.HitEye[1] = side < 0f ? 1f + 0.45f * side : 1f; (Present, lines 411-413), used in RenderBand at line 652: float hit = j.Hit * j.HitEye[eye];. In single-pass the centred quad shows the right-eye texture: eye.Picture = _tex[q == 0 ? 0 : 1]; (line 196) and LensEye.OnWillRenderObject shows only the centred quad when SinglePass (line 1196: show = Eye == 2;, SinglePass defined at line 1178).
  Trigger: In single-pass stereo (the mode the SinglePass branches exist for) a hit from the left (angle 90, HitDirX = -1) gives HitEye[1] = 0.55, and that is the picture the player actually sees. The red edge glows at 55% for a left hit and at 100% for the same hit from the right. Also, eye 0's picture (_tex[0]) is rendered on two of the four worker bands every frame in single-pass but never displayed, and the drops are offset by -2.5 px in the displayed picture (line 927).
  Change: In Present, when LensEye.SinglePass is true set j.HitEye[0] = j.HitEye[1] = 1f and use shift 0 for the drops (line 927). Also skip the eye-0 bands (bands 0 and 1 in WorkerLoop / RenderBand) in single-pass, since nothing reads _tex[0] then.
- [ ] [minor, effects-ui] src/Core/VRManager.cs:73: Failed VR setup leaves flat play with no notice and F8 dead; VRInput.Initialize outside try.
  What: _flatNotice = null; (line 63, before creation). bool inputReady = VRInput.Initialize(); (line 65) is outside the try (lines 66-78). catch (Exception e) { Log.Exception("Creating VR systems failed; shutting VR down", e); Shutdown(); yield break; } (lines 73-77). Retry gate: if (_flatNotice != null && !_retrying && UnityEngine.Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.F8)) (line 167).
  Trigger: If the new VRRig, WeaponSystem or UISystem throws, the game keeps running flat, the only trace is the log, and pressing F8 does nothing because _flatNotice was cleared at line 63. If VRInput.Initialize throws, the exception escapes the coroutine after OpenVRRuntime.Start() (line 57) has succeeded, so SteamVR stays initialised and unpumped with no on-screen message.
  Change: Move VRInput.Initialize() into the try block. In the catch, after Shutdown(), call FlatNotice("VR could not be set up: " + e.Message + "\nPress F8 to try again.") so the F8 retry path is re-armed.
- [ ] [minor, effects-ui] src/Rig/LensEffects.cs:160: Half-built LensEffects stays as Instance, so flash lists grow unpruned.
  What: Instance = this; (line 160) runs before the steps that can throw (lines 170-199). _ok = true; is only at line 213. Present() returns at lines 317-320 when !_ok, before the flash pruning at lines 329-341. The constructor's caller swallows the exception (VRRig.cs lines 110-117).
  Trigger: A throw while creating textures or quads (after line 160) leaves Instance pointing at this object with _ok false. LensFlash.TryFlash (LensFlash.cs lines 200-201, 262) keeps calling AddFlash, which appends two entries per shot to lists nothing prunes, so memory grows for the rest of the session. Separately, LensFlash.UpdateGlow returns at lines 349-353 when Instance is null, before pruning _shots (pruning at lines 354-360), while OnShot appends to _shots on every shot (LensFlash.cs line 105). That is a slow leak in the shader-missing path.
  Change: Make the constructor reset Instance = null (and destroy what was created) on any exception. In LensFlash.UpdateGlow, prune _shots before the null return. In TryFlash, also require the fx instance to be _ok before AddFlash.
- [ ] [minor, effects-ui] src/UI/CanvasManager.cs:119: Role change on an already-visible canvas skips re-flatten and keeps a stale menu pose.
  What: if (opening || visible && (Time.unscaledTime >= t.NextRoleCheck || gameplayChanged)) { ... t.Role = gameplay ? CanvasRole.Hud : RoleOutsidePlay(t.Canvas); } (lines 119-126). Only the opening path resets placement and flattening: if (opening && t.Role == CanvasRole.Menu) { t.Placed = false; CollectFlat(t); } (lines 127-131). The HUD branch in BeforeRender (lines 431-440) never clears t.Placed.
  Trigger: A canvas that stays visible changes role (menu to HUD and back) without reopening. For example, a menu whose interactable Selectables are briefly all off while still shown, or a HUD canvas that gains a clickable element. On the way back to menu, Placed is still true, so it reuses its old LocalPose (lines 444-449): the menu reappears where it was last placed relative to the body, not in front of the player, and its deep pages are not flattened again. Low confidence on how often the role flips.
  Change: Treat any role change like an opening: when t.Role differs from its previous value, set t.Placed = false, and for Menu call CollectFlat(t).
- [ ] [minor, effects-ui] src/Weapons/LensFlash.cs:394: World muzzle glow is not gated on death (the lens glow is).
  What: if (LensEffects.CoreInWorld) { _fireball.SetGlow(muzzle, _gunAhead, glowMost); } (lines 392-395), where glowMost comes from shown (lines 377-390) with no death check. The lens path gates death: j.Glow[e] = dead ? 0f : _glow[e]; (LensEffects.cs line 391).
  Trigger: The player dies while holding an automatic trigger. The lens glow drops to zero at once, but the world fireball glow keeps being placed at the muzzle for about 0.5 s (until the last shot leaves the _shots window) plus its decay (about 0.1 s), as long as the muzzle transform stays active (for example a gun still visible after death).
  Change: Compute glowMost as 0 when GameContext.PlayerActor is null or dead (or gate shown in UpdateGlow the same way Present gates j.Glow), then call SetGlow with 0.
- [ ] [tiny, held] src/Weapons/GunCollider.cs:394: UseShape clears the resting-prop ignore list but never re-applies it.
  What: `_restIgnored.Clear(); // the new pieces start colliding with everything: resting props are ignored again below` (394). Nothing below in UseShape (395-410) re-ignores them. The only re-ignore is UpdateRestingProps, which waits for `_nextRestCheck` (0.1 s; GunCollider.cs:761-767).
  Trigger: A draw where ChooseShape (HeldWeapon.cs:1431) swaps the hull into the stand-in: a magazine or weapon lying still within reach of the gun is shoved for up to 0.1 s.
  Change: Set `_nextRestCheck = 0f;` at the end of UseShape (or run the same re-ignore pass there) and correct the comment.
- [ ] [tiny, hands] src/Weapons/ArmIK.cs:99: Arm eases with clamped dt, glove with unclamped dt.
  What: ArmIK.cs:99: `_anchorOffset = Vector3.Lerp(_anchorOffset, anchorWant, 1f - Mathf.Exp(-(anchored ? 30f : 18f) * Mathf.Min(Time.unscaledDeltaTime, 0.05f)));`. The glove's equivalent is HandModel.cs:597, `_graspOffset = Vector3.Lerp(_graspOffset, want, 1f - Mathf.Exp(-(GraspAnchor.HasValue ? 30f : 18f) * dt));`, where dt = Time.unscaledDeltaTime (HandModel.cs:554) is not clamped.
  Trigger: A frame stall of about 100 ms (load, GC, hitch) while the off hand is anchored on a bolt handle. The glove reaches the anchor in one frame (exp(-3) left), but the arm moves only 1-exp(-1.5), about 78%, of its offset that frame. The arm trails by about 17% of the anchor shift (up to about 8 mm at the 5 cm clamp) for roughly 0.1 s, then catches up.
  Change: Use the same time step on both sides. Either drop Mathf.Min(..., 0.05f) at ArmIK.cs:99, or clamp dt once in HandModel.Apply (line 554) and read that value on both sides.
- [ ] [tiny, effects-ui] src/UI/WristHud.cs:93: LineRenderer given a 49-point array while positionCount is n (verify in Unity).
  What: _ring.positionCount = n; _ring.SetPositions(_ringPoints); (lines 93-94), where _ringPoints is sized RingSegments + 1 = 49 (line 26) and n can be as small as 2 (line 87).
  Trigger: If Unity's SetPositions takes the array length rather than positionCount, a ring filled to about 10% would draw about 43 extra stale or zero points, which would show as a spike line to the ring's centre on the wrist. If Unity honours positionCount, there is no bug. Not verified against the Unity 2020.3 API here.
  Change: Keep a Vector3[] of exactly n entries (or call SetPosition(i, p) for each point) so the array length and positionCount always agree.

Results (2026-10-16 evening), in the order above:
- 249.1 HeldWeapon.cs:1922 (fire-mode variant drives the magazine): fixed by the lead (f8ed393): AmmoState.MagazineOf, so a variant on its parent's model reads and moves the parent's magazine.
- 249.2 WeaponDrops.cs:1077: fixed by the helper (2abbb8a): an own weapon whose slot is taken is kept as the only copy.
- 249.3 WeaponDrops.cs:852: fixed by the helper (2abbb8a): the six-cap turns the oldest kept weapon into an ordinary drop instead of deleting it.
- 249.4 CanvasManager.cs:429: fixed by the helper (69ae586): the aspect comes from the largest HUD canvas.
- 249.5 MovingIn, 249.6 cached grip section, 249.7 grip pop, 249.8 DepthVel seed, 249.9 null volume: fixed by the lead (254d1a9, bf8e0cb, 1787b91).
- 249.10 HeldWeapon.cs:889: fixed by the helper (6684bf0): a sub weapon kicks with its own rig and class.
- 249.11 HeldWeapon.cs:994 (scope steadying speed is filter lag / dt): open. It is real, but the fix changes how steadying feels (Task 230). The fix: an angle-per-dt hand speed, low-passed at about 2 Hz, driving the cutoff. Check it in the headset.
- 249.12 HeldWeapon.cs:135: fixed by the helper (6684bf0): the physics stand-in is destroyed on release and on a failed setup.
- 249.13 / 249.21 ArmIK: fixed by the lead (254d1a9, 1787b91).
- 249.14 ThrowAndMelee.cs:334: fixed by the helper (0583a2d): the pin pull is measured from the grab point.
- 249.15 LensEffects.cs:412: fixed by the helper (6f3c862): both eyes are weighted the same in single-pass (see 225).
- 249.16 VRManager.cs:73: fixed by the helper (81ce1d8): VRInput.Initialize is inside the try, and a failure shows the notice and re-arms F6.
- 249.17 LensEffects.cs:160: fixed by the helper (6f3c862, 13db87a).
- 249.18 CanvasManager.cs:119: fixed by the helper (69ae586).
- 249.19 LensFlash.cs:394: fixed by the helper (9e7c2e8): the world glow is off when the player is dead.
- 249.20 GunCollider.cs:394: fixed by the helper (0583a2d).
- 249.22 WristHud.cs:93: not real (positionCount limits what is drawn).
To check in the headset (helper): flashes under heavy CPU load (156716d), hit glow and water drops in each eye (6f3c862), menus that flip between HUD and menu (69ae586), the 7th weapon swap and an own rifle left over 90 s (2abbb8a).
