### For 251: bug hunt of 8 read-only Haiku finders, each finding checked by 2 Haiku skeptics (2026-10-16 evening)

Verdict: confirmed = both skeptics found it real in the code, plausible = one, refuted = neither (kept so it is not reported again). Owner: lead or helper by the file split above.

- [x] 251.1 (fixed: 119d8be) [confirmed, major, helper] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1170: WeaponArms: one resolved arm hides both gloves.
  What: BeforeRender does `if (_armIK != null && _armIK.Active) { _armIK.Solve(); SetHandModelsSuppressed(true); }` (1167-1171). ArmIK.Active is `_right.Valid || _left.Valid` (ArmIK.cs:38), but SetHandModelsSuppressed (2008-2016) sets Suppressed on both Rig.Hands. Each arm chain is independently null when its hand bone is missing (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:547-548, ArmChainOf returns null for a missing hand), so a one-arm weapon is possible. AutoTest.cs:2919 only exercises weapons that have both chains.
  Trigger: Hands = Weapon's arms, and a weapon whose model has a right hand chain but no left one (a one-handed gun). The off-hand glove is hidden for the whole hold and the off arm is never solved, so the off hand is invisible (or frozen in the arm's idle pose if its mesh exists). The mirror case (only the left chain resolves) hides the gun-hand glove.
  Fix: Expose RightValid => _right.Valid and LeftValid => _left.Valid from ArmIK. In HeldWeapon set Rig.Dominant.Model.Suppressed = arms.RightValid and Rig.Off.Model.Suppressed = arms.LeftValid, and clear both in the else branch at 1172-1175.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. ArmIK.Active is Right.Valid || Left.Valid (ArmIK.cs:38). HeldWeapon.cs:1167-1170 calls SetHandModelsSuppressed(true) whenever Active, and that loops over all Rig.Hands (2008-2016). HandModel.Apply then hides the glove (HandModel.cs:599). ArmChainOf returns null for a missing hand (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1714-1717, assigned at 547-548). The analyzer names a "right hand only" case (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:812). Nothing re-shows the off glove, and AutoTest.cs:2919 skips such weapons. Severity is overstated: WeaponArms is opt-in (VRConfig.cs:226, default VRHands). Better fix: The proposed fix is right in mechanism: expose RightValid/LeftValid and suppress each glove by its own chain's validity. Caveat: ArmRenderers is a whole-mesh list (WeaponRig.cs:284-298) that the active path never hides. If a one-arm model's arm mesh still carries the missing arm, the restored glove appears beside a frozen arm. Check that case in the headset, since the mesh is not split per arm.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. HeldWeapon.cs:1167-1171 calls SetHandModelsSuppressed(true) whenever ArmIK.Active, and Active is true with one valid chain (ArmIK.cs:38). SetHandModelsSuppressed (2008-2016) hits both Rig.Hands. Suppressed is written only at 1170 and 2120, so nothing un-suppresses the free glove. One-sided chains really arise: WeaponAnalyzer.cs:547-548 and ArmChainOf 1714-1717 return null per side, and 812 has a "right hand only" state. The trigger needs the opt-in Hands=WeaponArms (VRConfig.cs:226 defaults to VRHands) and a rig with one hand, e.g. a mod. Vanilla assets are not in the repo, so that is unverified. Real but narrower than "major". Better fix: The proposed fix is sound. Apply it inside the Active branch (HeldWeapon.cs:1167-1171) every frame: Rig.Dominant.Model.Suppressed = arms.RightValid and Rig.Off.Model.Suppressed = arms.LeftValid, each null-checked on Model. Dominant is the gun-arm hand (VRRig.cs:58) and Off is the support hand (VRRig.cs:60), which matches ArmIK's _right and _left. The else-branch clear is unreachable because Active is fixed at construction.
- [x] 251.2 (fixed: daccda3, 479f066) [confirmed, major, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponAnalyzer.cs:815: Support palm near the grip sets IsPistol, shrinking bolt travel to 7 cm and disabling the AR and named handle rules.
  What: AnalyzeHands: `if (Vector3.Dot(d.SupportPoint - d.GripPoint, d.AimAxis) < 0.07f) { d.IsPistol = true; }`. IsPistol is then read as a handgun test by MaxBoltTravel (line 1763: `d.IsPistol ? 0.07f : 0.23f`), AnalyzeSlide's travel default (1141: pistol 0.035), ChargingHandleFromReload (1790 via MaxBoltTravel), ArChargingHandle (763 returns on IsPistol), NamedChargingHandle (581 returns on IsPistol) and DetectHinge revolverCandidate (1540). The comment at WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2630 confirms rifles with a support hand on the magazine well get this flag.
  Trigger: A non-pistol rifle whose left-hand palm sits within 7 cm forward of the grip (left hand on the magazine well in the idle pose). Its bolt carrier, which travels 8-23 cm on fire, fails `m > MaxBoltTravel(d)` (line 1061), so the slide is not found. The reload fallback also rejects the travel (line 1123), so SlideTravel falls to the pistol default of 3.5 cm (1141). The AR charging handle and named handle are skipped. A non-auto rifle with 4-9 rounds also becomes a revolver candidate (1540) and can be classed BreakOpen by the cylinder-name fallback (1617-1628).
  Fix: Add a separate `public bool Handgun` to WeaponRigData, set from `w.slot == 1` in AnalyzeHands (line 791), and use it in MaxBoltTravel, the ArChargingHandle and NamedChargingHandle guards, and revolverCandidate. Keep IsPistol for the grip and hand-pose consumers (HeldWeapon.cs 228/596/682, HandModel.WeaponIsPistol) and for the support-cup case.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code. AnalyzeHands (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:813-816) sets IsPistol when the support palm is under 0.07 along the bore, and nothing resets it (only assignments are at 235, 791, 815). AnalyzeHands runs before AnalyzeSlide (525 vs 530). MaxBoltTravel (1763) gates the fire bolt at 1061 and the reload fallback at 1123, so the default travel at 1141 becomes 0.035. ArChargingHandle (763), NamedChargingHandle (581) and revolverCandidate (1540) also key on the flag. The author's comments at 2630-2631 (rifles with a support hand on the well get "pistol") and 1761-1762 (rifle carriers reach 23 cm) show this is unintended, and the trigger is plausible. Minor overstatements: the test is "< 0.07", so a palm behind the grip also matches, and a name-matched slide is still found. Better fix: The proposed Handgun flag is right but incomplete. Set it from w.slot == 1 in both AnalyzeHands (791) and Fallback (235). Use it for gun-type checks, not only the four listed: MaxBoltTravel 1763; AnalyzeSlide travel default and clamp 1141-1142; RackStrokeFromHands guard and clamps 1348 and 1368; ArChargingHandle 763; NamedChargingHandle 581; BoltInsideOnSurface 641; SideKnobByName 656; ForwardSideHandle 716; revolverCandidate 1540; IsMachineGun 1660; FinishVirtualParts 3081, 3083 and 3094 (3094 sets LockOnEmpty for any self-loading gun without a slide when IsPistol is set). Keep IsPistol for grip and hand geometry (236-237, 799, 809-810, 1031, 2348, 3074, 3090) and for the HeldWeapon and HandModel consumers. Check the behaviour change for slot-0 guns held with a support cup: they would move from pistol bounds to rifle bounds.
  Skeptic (real): Real. AnalyzeHands sets IsPistol from palm position alone (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:813-815). Nothing resets it, and it runs before AnalyzeSlide (:530), NamedChargingHandle (:542) and ArChargingHandle (:562). Consumers 1061, 1123, 1141, 581, 763 and 1540 behave as claimed. Reach is documented: the :2630 comment and changed.md describe rifles whose support hand sits on the magwell getting the flag. Frequency is unmeasured. Nuance: a named bolt is still found at 1111 but gets 3.5 cm travel; only an unnamed one is lost. Better fix: Add Handgun = (w.slot == 1) and use it in MaxBoltTravel, the ArChargingHandle and NamedChargingHandle guards, and revolverCandidate. The proposed list also misses other IsPistol reads that affect rifles: 1141-1142 (default and clamp travel), 1348 and 1368 (hand-stroke early return and clamp), 1031 (eye offset in AnalyzeSights), 1660 (IsMachineGun), and 3083 and 3094 (virtual travel and LockOnEmpty). Keep IsPistol for grip and hand-pose consumers (HeldWeapon 228/596/682/741, HandModel.WeaponIsPistol).
- [x] 251.3 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/MagazineProp.cs:104: Palmed magazine keeps its partner's stale keep-out offset on the rail.
  What: The OnRail setter (lines 101-115) never clears `_keptOut`. `_keptOut` is written only in KeepOffGun (line 1774), which runs only from FollowHand, and reset only in Attach (line 1228). ReloadInteractions.BeforeRender adds `first.KeptOut` to the palmed magazine's pose (ReloadInteractions.cs:1725) whenever a palmed magazine exists, without checking `first.OnRail`.
  Trigger: Tactical reload: the old magazine is palmed in the off hand (AttachPalmed, ReloadInteractions.cs:425) and the fresh magazine is the off hand's primary. Push the fresh magazine about 3 cm off the well's line, so its last in-hand frame is pressed back by the receiver and KeepOffGun leaves `_keptOut` non-zero. UpdateHeldProp then sets `prop.OnRail = true` (ReloadInteractions.cs:1104). While it sits on the rail, ShowOnRail runs instead of FollowHand, so `_keptOut` is never refreshed and the palmed magazine stays offset by that stale amount every frame. The hand's own KeepOut has already decayed, because HandModel.cs:631 honours KeepOut only one frame after KeepOutFrame. The palmed magazine therefore floats away from the palm until the fresh one is seated and destroyed. The offset is as large as the last keep-out push, typically millimetres to a few centimetres.
  Fix: In the OnRail setter, when `value` is true, set `_keptOut = Vector3.zero;`. On the rail nothing keeps the magazine out of the gun. This runs during UpdateHeldProp, before ReloadInteractions.BeforeRender reads KeptOut.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. _keptOut is written only in KeepOffGun (MagazineProp.cs:1774) and reset only in Attach (:1228). The OnRail setter (:101-115) never clears it, and KeepOffGun runs only when !OnRail (:1617-1630), so it stays stale on the rail. ReloadInteractions.cs:1725 adds first.KeptOut to the palmed pose unconditionally, after UpdateHeldProp sets OnRail (:1104, called from Update at :211). Trigger reachable: a blocked in-hand frame followed by lined rail entry (:1090). Better fix: Plain zeroing in the setter is not clearly right. The palmed magazine can still sit against the receiver while the fresh one is on the rail, so some offset may still be needed. It also snaps the palmed magazine by the stale amount in one frame at rail entry. Safer: keep the palmed offset but ease KeptOut toward zero over about 0.1 s after OnRail becomes true, or recompute the palmed magazine's own keep-out against the gun each frame.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed by code path, not run in play. MagazineProp.cs:1774 is the only writer of _keptOut and it is zeroed only in Attach (:1228). FollowHand skips KeepOffGun while OnRail (:1617), so the value freezes on the rail. ReloadInteractions.cs:1725 adds KeptOut to the palmed pose with no OnRail check. Palming defaults on (VRConfig.cs:269). Rail entry needs lateral under 3.5 cm (:1090) on a frame where the last in-hand KeepOffGun was non-zero, which the code allows. Visible effect is minor and lasts only while the fresh magazine sits on the rail. Better fix: Do not just zero _keptOut in the OnRail setter. The palmed pose at ReloadInteractions.cs:1737 is applied unblended, so zeroing it snaps the palmed magazine by the full offset in one frame, the same jump the setter's comment at MagazineProp.cs:108-110 was written to prevent. Instead ease the offset to zero over SwitchBlend: make the KeptOut getter lerp _keptOut toward zero using _switchAt while OnRail, so the palmed magazine and the fresh one both leave the offset together.
- [x] 251.4 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/AmmoState.cs:332: ChamberPlusOne off: shell and tube loading overfills the tube past capacity.
  What: ShellChunk computes `int room = Capacity - MagRounds;` (AmmoState.cs:332) and LoadRounds computes the same room (AmmoState.cs:278). TakeFromPouch's internal-tube branch also uses `Mathf.Min(Ammo.Capacity - Ammo.MagRounds, ...)` (ReloadInteractions.cs:975). None of them subtract the chambered round. RoundsForFreshMagazine (AmmoState.cs:322) is the only place that does, and only when VRConfig.ChamberPlusOne is off (VRConfig.cs:213-214 says the chamber counts on top of a full magazine only when it is on).
  Trigger: ChamberPlusOne=false. A ShellFed pump or a Manual internal-tube gun with configuration.ammo 8, one round chambered and a tube of 7 (total 8). Take a shell from the pouch: ShellChunk returns 1, SeatProp's LoadRounds accepts 1, so MagRounds becomes 8 and Loaded becomes 9. The counter reads 8+1 and Weapon.ammo goes to 9, one above the configured total. Each further shell adds another round.
  Fix: Add `int TubeRoom => Capacity - MagRounds - (!VRConfig.ChamberPlusOne.Value && Chambered ? 1 : 0);` to AmmoState and use it in ShellChunk (line 332), LoadRounds (line 278) and TakeFromPouch's internal-tube branch (ReloadInteractions.cs:975).
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed overfill: ShellChunk (AmmoState.cs:332), LoadRounds (:278) and the pouch's internal-tube branch (ReloadInteractions.cs:975) ignore the chamber when ChamberPlusOne is off (VRConfig.cs:213). The only OFF-conditioned chamber reserve is RoundsForFreshMagazine (AmmoState.cs:322). The claimed stacking is wrong: once MagRounds equals Capacity the room is 0, so the excess stays at one round. The fix also misses InsertMagazine (AmmoState.cs:234, reached via ReloadInteractions.cs:1394), which clamps to Capacity; swapping in a full magazine while a round is chambered reaches the same 9-of-8. Better fix: Apply the proposed TubeRoom at AmmoState.cs:278 and :332 and ReloadInteractions.cs:975. Also add the same chamber reserve to InsertMagazine's clamp at AmmoState.cs:234: Mathf.Clamp(rounds, 0, Capacity - (!VRConfig.ChamberPlusOne.Value && Chambered ? 1 : 0)). The excess already goes to ReturnSpare there. Reword the finding: the overfill is capped at one round and does not stack.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed reachable in default Manual reload with ChamberPlusOne off. Spawn gives chamber+7 (AmmoState.cs:104-105). Tube room ignores the chamber at AmmoState.cs:332, :278, and ReloadInteractions.cs:975. AcceptsNow (ReloadInteractions.cs:1027) admits the shell, and nothing clamps Weapon.ammo (Weapon.cs:1473). Overstated: it does not grow per shell. Room hits 0 at 8+1, so the excess stays at Capacity+1. Better fix: Add to AmmoState: public int TubeRoom => Mathf.Max(0, Capacity - MagRounds - (!VRConfig.ChamberPlusOne.Value && Chambered ? 1 : 0)); Use it in ShellChunk (AmmoState.cs:332), LoadRounds (AmmoState.cs:278), TakeFromPouch's internal-tube branch (ReloadInteractions.cs:975), and the AcceptsNow default branch (ReloadInteractions.cs:1027, becoming return Ammo.TubeRoom > 0 && (!Breaks || Ammo.Open);). The Mathf.Max(0, ...) is needed because Unity's Mathf.Clamp(1, 0, -1) returns -1, which would subtract rounds from the tube.
- [x] 251.5 (fixed: the rocket rail work (250)) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:1207: Loosely lined rocket snaps into the tube mouth, up to 12 cm from the hand.
  What: LooselyLined accepts a tail up to 12 cm before the mouth (`along > -0.12f`, line 1207), but its out-param is `depth = Mathf.Max(0f, along);` (line 1204). The caller stores the difference as the hand offset (line 1098 `prop.RailHandOffset = along - looseDepth`) and rests the rail at the clamped depth (lines 1100 and 1105). A magazine or rocket on the rail is drawn at RailPose (line 1744), which uses that clamped depth, while the offset (line 1132 `along -= prop.RailHandOffset`) keeps the difference for as long as it stays on the line. The comment at lines 1094-1096 says it goes onto the line 'as it is'.
  Trigger: A rocket held like a stick in front of a launcher, its tail 3 to 12 cm outside the tube mouth, lateral under 12 cm, angle under 60 degrees. The strict test (line 1090) fails because along is below -0.03, and the loose test lines it up. The rocket jumps into the mouth by that distance and is then drawn that far deeper than the hand's tail for as long as it stays on the line. The strict path has the same clamp for along in (-0.03, 0), giving up to a 3 cm jump.
  Fix: Make the loose window end at the rail's own release limit: change `along > -0.12f` to `along > -0.03f` at line 1207. The release test at line 1136 uses the same -0.03 boundary, so the clamp then never hides a tail outside the mouth.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. LooselyLined (ReloadInteractions.cs:1198-1208) accepts along in (-0.12, -0.03] with depth clamped to 0 (1204, 1207). The caller (1092-1101) sets RailHandOffset = along - 0 and RailDepth = 0 (1105). ShowOnRail(RailPose) draws it at that depth with no smoothing (1744, MagazineProp.cs:1551, 1864). The offset persists through 1132, so the rocket stays up to 12 cm deeper than the hand's tail. The strict path only gives a non-persistent 3 cm snap, since its offset is 0. However, the proposed -0.03 fix reverts the 12 cm window added on purpose in commit d077bdd (Tasks 231) and documented at 1191-1196. Better fix: Do not shrink the window to -0.03; that removes the 12 cm acceptance commit d077bdd added deliberately. Keep -0.12 and remove the snap instead. For a loose grab, skip the depth clamp so RailDepth = along (negative allowed; RailPose already handles it) and keep RailHandOffset = 0. Use a loose-specific release limit near -0.12 in place of the -0.03 test at line 1136, and clear the flag once RailDepth > 0. Check the clamps at 1145 and 2048 and the inTube test at 1135 for negative depth.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. The loose test (ReloadInteractions.cs:1207) accepts tail along in (-0.12, -0.03], which the strict test (:1090) rejects, so a rocket 3-12 cm outside the mouth, aligned, is taken. The caller sets RailHandOffset = along - 0 and clamps depth to 0 (:1098-1105). The drawn pose uses the clamped RailDepth (MagazineProp.cs:1867, drawn at ReloadInteractions.cs:1744), while :1132 keeps the offset, so the rocket and its glove stay up to 12 cm deeper than the hand for the whole stroke. Nothing earlier guards this path. The reviewer's -0.03 fix does not remove the effect: in (-0.03, 0) the loose path still clamps with a nonzero offset. Better fix: Changing :1207 to along > -0.03f (matching the release test at :1136) caps the snap and the persistent offset at 3 cm. It does not make the clamp "never hide a tail outside the mouth", so that claim is wrong. Removing the mismatch fully needs the loose path to keep RailHandOffset at 0 and let RailDepth go down to -0.03 (the clamps at :1105 and :1145 are the ones to change for rockets), then check the other RailDepth readers such as :2182 and :2216 before relying on it.
- [x] 251.6 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:3490: Release() drops a palmed magazine whose GameObject a scene change already destroyed.
  What: Release() calls `_palmed.Drop(off.ThrowVelocity(), off.AngularVelocity);` (lines 3488-3490) with no `Go == null` check. Drop reaches `SetLayer(Go, ...)` (MagazineProp.cs:1894), which throws on a destroyed GameObject. The only cleanup that clears a dead _palmed, UpdatePalmed's `if (_palmed.Go == null) _palmed = null;` (line 1716), runs from BeforeRender. WeaponSystem skips that call when Held.Weapon is null (WeaponSystem.cs:262). ReleaseHeld runs first in the same frame (WeaponSystem.cs:113-116).
  Trigger: A tactical reload palms the old magazine, then the level unloads, or the held weapon is destroyed, before the fresh magazine is seated. ReleaseHeld calls Release, which throws. Log.Guard (WeaponSystem.cs:321) logs it, and the rest of Release is skipped. That includes `off.Model.Palming = false` and `off.Model.GraspAnchor = null` (lines 3513-3516), so the off-hand model keeps Palming set until another weapon is drawn and its BeforeRender resets it.
  Fix: Take the reference first and drop only live props: `MagazineProp palmed = _palmed; _palmed = null; if (palmed.Go != null) palmed.Drop(off.ThrowVelocity(), off.AngularVelocity);`. Move the `off.Model.Palming = false` reset above that block so it always runs.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. ReloadInteractions.cs:3492-3496 calls _palmed.Drop with no Go check. Drop reaches SetLayer(Go) at MagazineProp.cs:1894, which throws on a destroyed Go. The palmed prop's Go is a non-DontDestroyOnLoad root object. WeaponSystem.OnSceneChanged (WeaponSystem.cs:801-807) calls ReleaseHeld, and CanvasManager.cs:553 shows old-scene objects are already null by then. The finding's Log.Guard claim is wrong: HeldWeapon.cs:2075-2083 catches the exception first and logs "Releasing reload state". The skipped Palming/GraspAnchor reset is still real. Severity minor is fair. Better fix: In ReloadInteractions.Release (around line 3492), replace the palmed block with: MagazineProp palmed = _palmed; _palmed = null; if (palmed != null && palmed.Go != null) palmed.Drop(off.ThrowVelocity(), off.AngularVelocity); Also move the existing `if (off.Model != null) { off.Model.GraspAnchor = null; off.Model.Palming = false; }` block (around lines 3517-3521) above that palmed block, so it always runs. The finding's proposed off.Model.Palming move needs that null check. FinishEject (ReloadInteractions.cs:485), PromotePalmed (:444) and UpdateEject (:466) already guard on Go == null, so this matches the codebase's own pattern.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code: Release() (ReloadInteractions.cs:3492-3496) calls palmed.Drop with no Go check, and Drop reaches SetLayer (MagazineProp.cs:1894, 1201), which throws on a destroyed object. Palmed magazines are unparented roots (MagazineProp.cs:471, 757), so a held weapon's destruction alone is not a trigger. Level unload is: OnSceneChanged (WeaponSystem.cs:801-805) releases before DestroyAll, so it throws if Unity destroyed level objects first. Likely for Single loads (LoadingScreen.cs:232,239), not runtime-verified. Impact: one logged exception, stale GraspAnchor (glove drifts up to 5 cm) until next weapon draw. Better fix: MagazineProp palmed = _palmed; _palmed = null; if (off.Model != null) { off.Model.GraspAnchor = null; off.Model.Palming = false; } if (palmed != null && palmed.Go != null) palmed.Drop(off.ThrowVelocity(), off.AngularVelocity); A dead palmed prop is then freed by MagazineProp.Cull (MagazineProp.cs:2020-2024). Remove the weapon-destroyed trigger from the report: only a level unload reaches this path. The guard also stops the exception from skipping EndRack/EndCover/EndBelt/EndOptic.
- [x] 251.7 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/ArmIK.cs:92: WeaponArms off arm ignores the held magazine's palm and keep-out.
  What: For the off hand, `at = off.PalmCentre` (92) plus only the anchor offset. The arm never reads HandModel.HeldPalm (HandModel.cs:431, blended at 586-598) or HandModel.KeepOut (421, 625). onGun (85) is false for HandRole.Magazine, so the free target is the tracked palm. The glove that would follow the magazine onto the well line is hidden by Suppressed (HeldWeapon.cs:1170).
  Trigger: WeaponArms mode, reloading a magazine with the off hand while it slides along the well line (MagazineProp.cs:1587-1588 sets HeldBy.Model.HeldPalm). The arm's hand stays on the controller while the magazine sits on the well line, offset by the difference between the controller and the well-line palm, and the arm is not kept out of the receiver.
  Fix: Use the post-blend, post-follow target that HandModel.Apply computes (a helper on HandModel returning the target after HeldPalm blending and FollowOffset) in ArmIK.Solve for the off hand, and add HandModel.KeepOut to `at` when KeepOutFrame is fresh.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. ArmIK.cs:85 and 92 set the off target from off.PalmCentre (tracked palm) with no HeldPalm or KeepOut term. Magazines set no GraspAnchor (ReloadInteractions.cs:1763, switch at 1800-1806 has no Magazine anchor), so the correction at ArmIK.cs:99 is zero. Arm mode hides the glove (HeldWeapon.cs:1182-1183). The well-line palm is set at MagazineProp.cs:1587, and the magazine stays on the rail up to 7 cm lateral (ReloadInteractions.cs:1136). Nuance: ArmIK does read HeldPalm indirectly through GraspPointNow, but that delta is zero for magazines. Default hands mode is VRHands (VRConfig.cs:226), so this is opt-in. Better fix: Expose HandModel.PlacedTarget() (HandModel.cs:558) and use its position in place of off.PalmCentre at ArmIK.cs:92. At default HandModelPosition it equals PalmCentre exactly (HandModel.cs:547, VRHand.cs:194), so unheld behaviour is unchanged. Also add the eased private _keepOut (HandModel.cs:423, updated at 632), not the raw KeepOut target, or the arm snaps up to 12 cm when keep-out starts. Caveat: Solve runs before Reload.BeforeRender (HeldWeapon.cs:1182 vs 1190), so HeldPalm and KeepOut read there are one frame stale. Move the arm solve after the reload pass or accept the one-frame lag.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed reachable. WeaponArms is opt-in (VRConfig.cs:226, default VRHands). With it on, the glove is hidden (HeldWeapon.cs:1170) and the arm's palm is the tracked PalmCentre (ArmIK.cs:92). A magazine on the rail keeps HandRole.Magazine (MagazineProp.cs:1230), so onGun is false (ArmIK.cs:85; TwoHanded is cleared on grip release, ReloadInteractions.cs:196). Its HeldPalm is set at MagazineProp.cs:1587 and used only by HandModel.PlacedTarget. GraspAnchor is never set for Magazine (ReloadInteractions.cs:1807-1817), so the anchor delta at ArmIK.cs:99 is zero. The rail lets the magazine drift up to 7 cm laterally (ReloadInteractions.cs:1136). Commit 1787b91 and Task 224 fixed only the glove. Severity: minor, opt-in visual only. Better fix: Do not substitute the glove's pose into the arm: PlacedTarget includes the HandModelPosition/rotation offset, which is not in the arm's palm mapping. Instead, add an internal HandModel helper returning the delta (PlacedTarget() minus TrackedTarget(), in position and rotation). Include the eased private _keepOut field, not the raw KeepOut, to avoid a jump, and only when KeepOutFrame is fresh. In ArmIK.Solve for the off hand, add the position delta to `at` and apply the rotation delta to `rot` before computing `free`. Also correct the comment at HandModel.cs:555-556, which says one function serves the glove and the arm; ArmIK only uses GraspPointNow for the anchor delta.
- [x] 251.8 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Rig/HandModel.cs:714: Finger solve reuse tolerates up to 6 mm, not the documented 3 mm.
  What: GripMoved (976-989) compares against _slideAtPos and updates that reference only when it reports moved (987-988). Finger reuse (714-716) requires only !moved, so the hand may be anywhere within 3 mm of the reference. The cached solve (747-752) was computed at the position the hand had when the curl last changed, which can be up to 3 mm from the reference on the other side. The palm target is recomputed on moved frames, so only the fingers drift.
  Trigger: Hold a measured gun and let the trigger settle so that the curl stops changing and a solve is cached at P1. The hand then drifts about 3 mm one way and back past the reference with no curl change: the fingers keep the P1 solve, about 6 mm away from the real hand position, so they sink or float by up to 6 mm into the held solid.
  Fix: At the cache write (747-752) also store _root.position and _root.rotation. Add a reuse condition that the root is within about 1 mm of the stored solve position, instead of comparing against the search reference.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. Reuse (HandModel.cs:721-724) tests only !moved, and GripMoved (977-996) moves its reference only on moved frames. Non-moved frames still re-solve and cache (730-759) at the current root, which Chain (1113-1116) reads through _root.localToWorldMatrix. A cache can sit 3 mm from the reference and the hand can drift 3 mm back past it, about 6 mm total. Rotation has the same gap. Live for the main gun hand (HeldWeapon.cs:1708; delegate is cached at HeldWeapon.cs:1287, so the ReferenceEquals check is stable). Better fix: The proposed fix is incomplete. Store the root pose per finger at the cache write (HandModel.cs:754-759), since the cache is per finger, and compare the reuse test against that stored pose instead of _slideAtPos. Include rotation, which the proposed fix leaves out. Use 3 mm and 2 degrees to match the documented tolerance, or 1 mm if a tighter bound is wanted. Tighter thresholds mean more re-solves while the hand jitters, so the per-frame cost should be checked.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code, narrow in play. Reuse (HandModel.cs:721-723) gates only on !moved. moved is measured against _slideAtPos (977-996), which changes only on moved frames. Caches are written at 754-759 on any non-moved frame where a curl, pull or thumb change forces a solve, so the cached root can be up to 3 mm from the reference, giving about 6 mm error. It is reached only when the weapon hand is not snapped (SnapPose null, root follows tracked hand plus FollowOffset lag). The cited line 714 is the thumb branch, not the reuse check. Severity minor. Better fix: Store the root pose the solve used at the cache write (HandModel.cs:754-759, not 747-752): _root.position and _root.rotation. Reuse only while the root stays within about 3 mm and 2 degrees of that stored pose, which keeps the documented tolerance and most reuse. A 1 mm window also works but re-solves more often. Keep the !moved gate for Solid and SolidVersion changes.
- [ ] (moved to 252 g: with the instant glove push-out it matters more now) 251.9 [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:1893: Keep-out uses last frame's hand and skin, applied this frame.
  What: KeepEmptyHandOut and KeepOutOfGun run in ReloadInteractions.BeforeRender (1668-1712, called from HeldWeapon.BeforeRender before the hand Apply per VRManager.cs:235-245). ShortestWayOut and ClearanceAlong take their base as `tracked = -_graspOffset - _keepOut` (HandModel.cs:469, 497), which is relative to the root set by the previous Apply, and test against last frame's _skinA/_skinB. The result is applied to this frame's target in Apply (633). The 249 fix moved the ArmIK grip point to this frame for the same ordering reason; the keep-out was left on the previous frame.
  Trigger: Push a magazine or the empty hand into the receiver at speed. The glove is searched one frame behind, so for that frame it sits inside the receiver by one frame of hand travel (about 5 mm at 0.5 m/s at 90 Hz) before the next frame corrects it.
  Fix: Use this frame's target as the base in both ClearanceAlong and ShortestWayOut: `Vector3 tracked = TrackedTarget().position - _root.position;` (the test then lands on this frame's glove). Alternatively run the keep-out search after the hand's Apply.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. ClearanceAlong and ShortestWayOut take their base and skin from the last Apply (HandModel.cs:469, 497, 525), and the keep-out search runs in Weapons.BeforeRender (ReloadInteractions.cs:1712, 1893) before the hand's Apply that applies it (VRManager.cs:235-241, HandModel.cs:640). Real but small: one frame of travel, and the eased _keepOut (HandModel.cs:632) already trails more. The proposed fix is wrong: during on-rail insertion HeldPalm moves the placed target off the tracked hand (MagazineProp.cs:1587, HandModel.cs:569), so TrackedTarget tests the wrong spot. Better fix: Do not use TrackedTarget. Compute this frame's placed target with this frame's HeldPalm and held-palm weight. PlacedTarget currently reads the last Apply's _heldPalmLast and _heldPalmWeight (HandModel.cs:608-610, 561-570), so it is one frame stale too. Evaluate it after ShowOnRail sets HeldPalm (ReloadInteractions.cs:1744), then use `placed.position - _root.position` as the base in both ClearanceAlong and ShortestWayOut. Otherwise accept the one-frame error as minor.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism holds: ClearanceAlong and ShortestWayOut base on last Apply's skin and offsets (HandModel.cs:469, 497; skin set at 776 and 784-802), and KeepOutOfGun runs before this frame's Apply (VRManager.cs:235-245). Reachable on a rail insert (ReloadInteractions.cs:1812) and an empty off hand (1805). Not corrected next frame: the lag recurs every frame the hand moves. The 30/s lerp (HandModel.cs:632) adds about 2.5 frames more. The proposed TrackedTarget base ignores HeldPalm (MagazineProp.cs:1587), so it regresses rail inserts. Better fix: Do not use TrackedTarget(). Store PlacedTarget().position where UpdateSkin runs (HandModel.cs:776), then in both ClearanceAlong and ShortestWayOut use tracked = (PlacedTarget().position - storedPlaced) - _graspOffset - _keepOut. That is the hand's movement since the skin was made, and it keeps the palm-push and slide offsets; the proposed _root.position base drops them. Also run the keep-out search after ShowOnRail (ReloadInteractions.cs:1744), because PlacedTarget uses HeldPalm only after Apply copies it (HandModel.cs:608).
- [-] (won't fix: overrides are rarely used; see below) 251.10 [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponOverrides.cs:140: Magazine, slide and pitch overrides change one field and keep the detected part's seat, bounds, eject axis and co-moving parts.
  What: Apply sets only `d.MagazinePath = WeaponRig.PathOf(w.transform, t);` (line 143) after analysis. MagSeat, MagBounds, MagEjectAxis, MagazinePartPaths, MagShownAtIdle, MagHideOnlyPaths, MagazineIsSkinned and MagProtrusion all stay from the detected magazine (WeaponAnalyzer.cs 2098-2159). Pitch (170-175) rotates AimAxis and Up only, so SlideTravel, MagEjectAxis and the sight data keep the old axis.
  Trigger: A user sets "magazine" in RavenfieldVR.weapons.json to the correct part. The rig's Magazine is the new transform, but the seat it slides into, its size and eject axis, and the detected rounds, follower and base plate (MagazinePartPaths, which hide with the magazine) still belong to the misdetected part. The override does not fix what it is meant to fix.
  Fix: When an override names a magazine, pass that transform into AnalyzeMagazine as the chosen part and skip detection, so every derived field is computed for it. For pitch, rotate the dependent vectors (SlideTravel, MagEjectAxis, sight lines) too, or apply it before those are derived.
  Skeptic (real): Verified. WeaponOverrides.cs:138-145 sets only MagazinePath, and Apply runs after Analyze (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:117, 125). MagSeat, MagEjectAxis, MagBounds, MagazinePartPaths and MagShownAtIdle stay from the detected part (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2098-2159). HeldWeapon.cs:1975 snaps the new magazine onto the old MagSeat when inserted, and WeaponRig.cs:305-324 hides the old co-movers with it. The pitch half is literal but over-broad: measured vectors (eye direction, WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1016-1022) should not rotate. Better fix: Force the overridden transform into AnalyzeMagazine (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1923) as the chosen part, skipping detection, so MagSeat, MagBounds, MagEjectAxis, MagazinePartPaths, MagazineIsSkinned, MagShownAtIdle and MagKeyframes are all derived for it. That also keeps it out of SparePartPaths, since CollectSpareMagazines excludes the chosen mag; HideSpareParts (HeldWeapon.cs:481-501) would otherwise hide it in hand. For pitch, do not rotate measured vectors (eye direction, animation-measured slide travel). Only the AimAxis/Up-derived fallbacks go stale (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1032, 2108, 3083, 3092), so either rotate just those or pitch the bore before the axis-dependent analysis.
  Skeptic (real): Magazine half confirmed. Apply runs after Analyze (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:117,125) and only sets MagazinePath (WeaponOverrides.cs:143). MagSeat, MagBounds, MagEjectAxis and part paths come from the detected part (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2098-2159); the sampler is disposed at :557, so nothing re-derives them. HeldWeapon.cs:1975 pins Rig.Magazine to the stale MagSeat under the default Manual reload (VRConfig.cs:211). Pitch half is only partly right: measured mesh vectors should not rotate. Better fix: Magazine: resolve the named transform inside Analyze before detection (before AnalyzeMagazine at WeaponAnalyzer.cs:531) so the seat, bounds, eject axis and co-parts are all derived for it. Apply cannot recompute them later because the sampler is disposed at :557. The no-magazine fallback (FinishVirtualParts, :3086-3093) must honour it too. Pitch: do not rotate SlideTravel from the slide's motion, MagEjectAxis from the pull, or measured sight lines. Re-derive only the AimAxis-based values: virtual SlideTravel (:3083), virtual eject (:3092), and EyeDirection = AimAxis (:882, :1032).
- [-] (log and comment corrected; an edit reaches weapons analysed after it) 251.11 [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponOverrides.cs:77: Override edits never reach a weapon already built this scene: WeaponSystem keeps the old rig per instance.
  What: EnsureLoaded: `WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache(); Log.Info("Weapon overrides changed: weapons are analysed again with them.");` with the comment 'weapons analysed already are analysed again (from their next draw)'. WeaponSystem.RigFor (lines 346-353) returns `_rigs[weapon]` for the same Weapon instance, so the next draw never calls WeaponAnalyzer.Get. _rigs is only cleared on a scene change (line ~807) or when the weapon is destroyed.
  Trigger: Edit RavenfieldVR.weapons.json during a match, then draw a weapon that was already held this scene. The old WeaponRig with the old overrides is used. The edit only reaches new Weapon instances or the next scene.
  Fix: Add a generation counter to WeaponAnalyzer, bumped in ClearCache, and have WeaponSystem.RigFor store the generation with each rig and rebuild when it is stale. Or correct the comment and the log message to match the real behaviour.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. WeaponSystem.RigFor (lines 346-354) returns the cached rig per Weapon instance, and WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache (line 138) empties only the static analysis cache, not _rigs, which is cleared only in OnSceneChanged (line 813). The comment at WeaponOverrides.cs:75-76 is false. The finding understates the problem: EnsureLoaded runs only through Apply on a cache miss (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:110-125), and WeaponWarmup pre-analyses every entry (WeaponWarmup.cs:141-162), so edits are often never read, even in a new scene. Better fix: A generation bump in ClearCache is not enough, because ClearCache runs only inside EnsureLoaded, which is reached only on a cache miss. Check the file stamp on every WeaponAnalyzer.Get, before the cache lookup, and bump the generation there. RigFor must rebuild stale rigs, and AmmoState.Rig (captured at creation, WeaponSystem.cs:416) must be refreshed too. The minimal honest fix is to correct the comment and the log at WeaponOverrides.cs:75-78 to say that edits apply after a game restart.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. WeaponSystem.RigFor (lines 346-353) returns the stored rig without calling WeaponAnalyzer.Get, and _rigs is cleared only in OnSceneChanged (line 813) or when a dead weapon is removed (line 238). ClearCache never touches _rigs. The finding also understates the bug: WeaponAnalyzer.Cache is static, keyed by prefab, and cleared only from EnsureLoaded, which runs only inside Apply on a cache miss (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:107-126). WeaponWarmup pre-analyses every entry (WeaponWarmup.cs:148), so an edit is mostly never noticed, even for new instances or after a scene change. Better fix: Run the file-stamp check on every lookup, not only on a cache miss: in WeaponSystem.RigFor and before the cache hit in WeaponAnalyzer.Get. When the stamp changed, call WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache() and bump a generation that each WeaponRig stores; RigFor rebuilds a rig whose generation is stale. A generation counter bumped only by ClearCache is not enough, because ClearCache only runs on a miss, and the warmup means misses almost never happen.
- [x] 251.12 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1660: Analysis reads the live fire mode, which FireSelector changes at runtime, but the cache key ignores it.
  What: `d.IsMachineGun = d.Hinge == HingeKind.FeedCover || c.auto && c.ammo >= 60 && !d.IsPistol;` with `Weapon.Configuration c = w.configuration;`. ClassifyAction (165-169), CyclesByHand (537) and DetectHinge (1538-1540) also read live `c.auto`. FireSelector.Toggle calls Weapon.SetSingleFire, which sets configuration.auto = false (Weapon.cs:686). RestoreSemi (FireSelector.cs:33-41, called from WeaponDrops.RestoreAmmo at WeaponDrops.cs:682) changes it on pickup. KeyOf (94-99) has no fire mode.
  Trigger: A cache miss for an automatic gun with a 60+ round box (LMG or big SMG) while its instance is in semi mode. The miss happens when the first attach comes before WeaponWarmup reaches that entry (WeaponWarmup's own comment says matches can start first) or after an override ClearCache. IsMachineGun is then false for that analysis and every later instance of the prefab, which loses the heavy handling and the longer box insertion.
  Fix: Analyse from the authored configuration: when the entry is known, read `entry.prefab.GetComponent<Weapon>().configuration` for ClassifyAction, CyclesByHand, DetectHinge and IsMachineGun, and fall back to the live one only without a prefab.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed: WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1660, 165-169, 537 and 1538-1540 read live configuration.auto, KeyOf (94-99) ignores fire mode, and SetSingleFire clears auto (Weapon.cs:686). The stated trigger is wrong, though. A first analysis sees semi only if a fire-mode press lands before AttachHeld (WeaponSystem.cs:117-119, GameInputBridge.cs:236). RestoreSemi (WeaponDrops.cs:685) needs a prior analysis. The reachable path: an override edit, detected on a later cache miss (WeaponOverrides.cs:77, 107-109), clears the cache, then a semi-restored instance is analysed first. Minor. Better fix: Read the authored values from the matching prefab child, not entry.prefab.GetComponent<Weapon>(). That returns the root for sub-weapons (WeaponWarmup.cs:154-158, HeldWeapon.cs:563), so map the live weapon by child path from its instance root (WeaponRig.PathOf / Resolve). Also cover the live reads the finding omits: WeaponAnalyzer.cs:221 and 227-228, and WeaponOverrides.cs:130-131. Fall back to live values only when there is no entry or prefab.
  Skeptic (real): The stated trigger (first attach of a semi instance before warmup) is unreachable: semi needs a prior toggle on an attached weapon (WeaponSystem.cs:303, 346-354 caches the key) or RestoreSemi on a copy made from one (WeaponDrops.cs:683-686, 825, 1017). The reachable route is WeaponOverrides.cs:77 ClearCache after a mid-session json edit, on a later Get miss. Then a pickup or holster copy (new instance, not in _rigs) is attached while semi and caches IsMachineGun=false for the session (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:1660). Narrow, so minor is fair. Better fix: Keep the authored-config idea, but state the real trigger (mid-session overrides edit, then ClearCache, then a restored semi copy attached first). Null-entry lookups need w.weaponEntry?.prefab. Weapon.configuration is a [Serializable] per-instance copy (Weapon.cs:86-87, 328), so the prefab's copy is authored. Simplest equivalent: bool authoredAuto = c.auto || FireSelector.IsSemiByPlayer(w); use it in ClassifyAction, CyclesByHand, DetectHinge and IsMachineGun.
- [x] 251.13 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2084: Rocket container rule takes any ancestor 15-160 cm as the rocket, without the moves-in-reload or rocket-mesh checks its comment promises.
  What: `if (size > 0.15f && size < 1.6f && index.ContainsKey(up)) { ...; mag = up; break; }` in the SingleShot branch (2076-2094). The comment at 2078-2080 says the container must move with the rocket in the reload, hold a rocket-sized mesh, and never be the launcher itself. Only the gunBody stop enforces the last point, and the size test is on the whole subtree's bounds.
  Trigger: A SingleShot launcher whose small rocket part (under 12 cm) sits under a small holder, with a non-moving launcher tube (15-160 cm) above it that is not the gunBody. The climb takes the tube. The reload then hides the tube as the magazine, and the rocket prop becomes a copy of the tube.
  Fix: Require the chosen ancestor to move in the reload (`sampler.ReloadMax[index[up]] >= 0.03f`) and to contain a mesh of rocket size (test the child meshes, not the whole subtree). Otherwise keep climbing or stop.
  Skeptic (real): Code matches the finding. WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2083-2084 accepts any ancestor 0.15-1.6 m with only index.ContainsKey, no ReloadMax or child-mesh check, though the comment at 2078-2080 promises both (commit 6febd4b). The path-1 climb (1993-2001) rejects still parts, but this SingleShot climb does not. The harm is partly real: CopiesModel (MagazineProp.cs:1087) allows a rocket copy of a tube under 1.4 m, and CopyVisuals copies rig.Magazine. The "reload hides the tube" step has no runtime code: UsesDetachableMag is false for SingleShot (AmmoState.cs:67) and MagazineToggles is diagnostic only. Narrow geometry, so minor. Better fix: Keep the proposed fix, tightened: in the SingleShot climb require sampler.ReloadMax[index[up]] >= 0.03f, test child renderer bounds (some single mesh's longest side in 0.15-1.6 m) instead of the subtree LocalBounds, and keep climbing otherwise. Drop the "reload hides the tube" wording; the actual harm is the rocket prop copying the tube.
  Skeptic (real): The code gap is real: WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2084 checks only size and index.ContainsKey, and the latter is always true for transforms under root (lines 275-280), so the first non-gunBody ancestor of 15-160 cm wins with no ReloadMax test. Nothing downstream rejects it: a tube-sized copy passes the fit check at MagazineProp.cs:644-645 against the analysed tube bounds. The trigger needs a moving sub-12 cm part, a sub-15 cm holder and a static tube. No shipped launcher in the repo is shown to have that shape, but the rule serves mod weapons. The hide step is wrong: HeldWeapon.cs:1947 hides magazine parts only for detachable mags, so the effect is a tube-sized pouch and hand rocket, not a hidden tube. Better fix: Do not just add ReloadMax >= 0.03: the scoped RPG-7V holder that this branch was written for may not move, and the fix would drop that working case back to the stand-in. Instead, accept an ancestor only if its own subtree holds a visible mesh whose bounds are rocket-sized (per-mesh, not the summed subtree bounds), and keep climbing past a static ancestor that is larger than a rocket. Add the movement test only after the scoped RPG-7V autotest line 'launcher: rocket from' shows the holder moves. Verify on RPG-7, RPG-7V and scoped RPG-7V.
- [x] 251.14 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponOverrides.cs:77: Editing the overrides file clears the analyser cache but not the hand volume or the held shape.
  What: On an overrides edit only WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache() runs (WeaponOverrides.cs:77). The measured hand volume lives in GunVolume's static Cache under rig.Data.Key (GunVolume.cs:284 and 319-352), and the held shape under "held:"+Key (GunCollider.cs:535, ShapeFit.cs:80-82). GunVolume has no clear method, and ShapeFit.ClearCache (ShapeFit.cs:648) is never called. The key does not change with the overrides (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:94-99). The overrides do change the aim frame: gripForward, gripUp and pitch (WeaponOverrides.cs:169-174), so the old volume and pieces sit in the old frame.
  Trigger: While the game runs, edit gripUp, gripForward or pitch for a weapon already measured this session, then draw it. HandSolid returns the cached volume and calls SetPose with the new grip and frame (HeldWeapon.cs:1280), so the hand solid is offset or rotated against the model. GunCollider.Create gets the old pieces (ShapeFit.cs:80-82), so the stand-in sits off the model until the game restarts.
  Fix: Add a GunVolume.ClearCache() that drops all entries, and call it together with ShapeFit.ClearCache() next to WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache() in WeaponOverrides.cs:77.
  Skeptic (real): Stale-cache part is real: WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:138) leaves GunVolume.Cache (GunVolume.cs:26, 319-352) and the "held:" ShapeFit entries (ShapeFit.cs:80-82) alone; ShapeFit.ClearCache (ShapeFit.cs:648) is never called. The stated trigger is wrong: EnsureLoaded only runs inside Apply (WeaponOverrides.cs:109), and Apply runs only on a cache miss (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:110-125), so drawing an already-analysed weapon never reads the file. Mismatch needs another weapon's miss, then a rig rebuild (WeaponSystem.cs:813 or a new Weapon instance) before the draw. Better fix: Run the overrides check on every WeaponAnalyzer.Get, before the cache-hit return (or from WeaponSystem.RigFor), not only from Apply on a miss. On a detected change, clear GunVolume's Cache and CacheOrder (new GunVolume.ClearCache clearing both), drop the "held:" ShapeFit entries (ShapeFit.ClearCache or per-key removal), and remove the affected WeaponSystem._rigs entries so the next draw rebuilds the rig with the new grip and frame. The proposed two-line change alone never runs for the stated trigger.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism is real, but the stated trigger is not. Overrides are re-read only inside Apply on an analyser cache miss (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:110-125, WeaponOverrides.cs:109), so redrawing an already-analysed weapon keeps grip, volume and pieces consistent. Mismatch needs: edit, a later miss for another weapon clearing the analyser cache (WeaponOverrides.cs:77), then the weapon re-analysed after a scene change (WeaponSystem.cs:813, VRManager.cs:257) and held, so stale GunVolume.cs:284 and ShapeFit.cs:80-82 entries pair with new grip and frame. Warm-up makes misses rare, not impossible. Better fix: Keep the proposed clears, but GunVolume.ClearCache must empty CacheOrder as well as Cache, or CacheOrder keeps duplicate and stale keys. Call GunVolume.ClearCache and ShapeFit.ClearCache in WeaponOverrides.EnsureLoaded's changed branch, next to WeaponAnalyzer.ClearCache (WeaponOverrides.cs:77). Correct the trigger in the finding: a plain redraw applies nothing, and the mismatch needs an intervening analyser miss plus a re-analysis in a new scene.
- [x] 251.15 (fixed: 3702cf7) [confirmed, minor, helper] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1266: Carried infinite-ammo guns never get a hand solid.
  What: HandSolid gates measurement with `Rig.Data.Action != ActionType.NoAmmo` (line 1266). NoAmmo is assigned to any weapon with `configuration.ammo <= 0` (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:153-155), not only to kits. HeldWeapon.cs:909-911 says an infinite-ammo gatling is a drawn gun whose cases are ejected. For these guns `_volume` stays null, so HandSolid returns null (line 1271), SnapHands sets `main.Model.Solid = null` (line 1705), and the foregrip/support solids are null too (lines 1729-1743). The gloves then have no gun solid and can close through the model. The kit case this seems meant for is already covered by `_isKit` (line 1698 sets HiddenByHeld, so no glove is drawn). The exclusion was added in 7166552 without a comment, and it conflicts with that commit's stated aim ('Gloves no longer clip into held weapons').
  Trigger: Draw and hold a weapon whose configuration.ammo is 0 (the infinite-ammo gatling the comment at HeldWeapon.cs:909-911 describes). Its gun hand and support hand are not kept out of the drawn model for the whole hold.
  Fix: Replace `Rig.Data.Action != ActionType.NoAmmo` at line 1266 with `!_isKit`, so only the kits that hide their glove are skipped.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code, but the gate is at HeldWeapon.cs:1284, not 1266. Infinite ammo is configuration.ammo == -1 (Weapon.cs:2086-2088), and ClassifyAction maps ammo <= 0 to NoAmmo (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:153-155). The gate is the only path to GunVolume.For (1287), so _volume stays null and HandSolid returns null (1289). SnapHands then sets both gloves' Solid to null (1723, 1761), and HandModel.cs:864 Closing requires Solid != null. Added in 7166552 with no comment. Kits are handled by _isKit (1716). Better fix: Change HeldWeapon.cs:1284 (not 1266) to `&& !_isKit`. Kits are still skipped, and their glove stays hidden through HiddenByHeld (1716). Non-kit NoAmmo guns then get a volume, as Throwable and Melee already do. Note that GunCollider.cs:512 groups NoAmmo with tools and kits, so non-kit tools will also be measured; that matches existing Throwable and Melee handling. Severity minor. The "close through the model" wording is loose: with no solid, the fingers are simply not solved against the gun.
  Skeptic (real): Code path confirmed, with wrong line numbers: the gate is HeldWeapon.cs:1284 (1266 is InsideDrawnGun). Only configuration.ammo <= 0 maps to NoAmmo (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:153-155), and for a gun that is the game's infinite magazine (configuration.ammo == -1, Weapon.cs:2086-2088). The author's own notes name an infinite-ammo gatling as a drawn gun (changed.md:625, HeldWeapon.cs:925-927). GunVolume.For is only called at :1287, so _volume stays null, Solid stays null, and Closing is false (HandModel.cs:864). No fallback exists. The trigger text "ammo is 0" is wrong: it is -1. Better fix: Replace the NoAmmo test at HeldWeapon.cs:1284 with `!_isKit && (Rig.Data.Action != ActionType.NoAmmo || Weapon.configuration != null && Weapon.configuration.ammo == -1)`. This measures only infinite-magazine guns and leaves ammo-0 tools as they are. A plain `!_isKit` also fixes the gun, but it also starts measuring non-kit ammo-0 tools, which the original exclusion may have been avoiding.
- [x] 251.16 (fixed: edd0bba) [confirmed, minor, helper] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:1268: One failed gun measurement disables the hand solid for the whole hold.
  What: `_volumeTried = true;` is set before `_volume = GunVolume.For(Rig, frame, Rig.Data.GripPoint);` (lines 1268-1269), and the gate on line 1266 requires `!_volumeTried`. GunVolume.For returns null without caching when Build returns null: no enabled mesh renderers (VisibleRenderers), a degenerate box after the envelope clamp (GunVolume.cs:427-431), or an exception caught in Cached (GunVolume.cs:336-342). GunVolume.cs:342 says a null result is 'tried again the next time it is held', but within this HeldWeapon that never happens, because it is gated by _volumeTried. HandSolid then returns null (line 1271) until the weapon is holstered.
  Trigger: The first attempt (at rest, 0.25 s or more after the draw) gets null from GunVolume.For, for example a briefly disabled renderer or an exception in Build. The gun then has no hand solid for that entire hold, although a retry a second later would have worked.
  Fix: Latch only on success: `GunVolume v = GunVolume.For(...); if (v != null) _volume = v; else _volumeRetryAt = Time.unscaledTime + 1f;`, and gate line 1266 on `Time.unscaledTime >= _volumeRetryAt` instead of `!_volumeTried`.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed: HeldWeapon.cs:1284-1287 sets _volumeTried before GunVolume.For, so a null leaves _volume null and HandSolid returns null for that HeldWeapon (new one per attach, WeaponSystem.cs:307). The example trigger is wrong: VisibleRenderers (GunCollider.cs:572-600) returns only enabled renderers and HeldWeapon never disables every body renderer. The degenerate-box null (GunVolume.cs:471-474) is deterministic at rest. Only a thrown exception (GunVolume.cs:373-376) is plausibly transient. Cited line numbers are off; the cache comment is at GunVolume.cs:379. Better fix: Keep the proposed latch-on-success retry, but cap it (about 3 attempts, 1 s apart) because deterministic nulls would otherwise retry every second. Also cover the async failure path, which the proposed fix misses: GunVolume.cs:648 and :680 set Volume.Failed after assignment, and HandSolid (HeldWeapon.cs:1289) never reassigns _volume, so a failed read-back or decode blanks the hand solid for the whole hold. Reset _volume to null when _volume.Failed is seen, then apply the same bounded retry.
  Skeptic (real): Latch is real: HeldWeapon.cs:1284-1287 sets _volumeTried before GunVolume.For, and nothing resets it. HeldWeapon is per draw (WeaponSystem.cs:307). For returns null on empty renderers (GunVolume.cs:442), a degenerate box (:473) or a caught exception (:373). A transient null needs the gun hidden at that moment (photo mode, Weapon.Hide) or a thrown exception; ladder, seat and death release Held first (WeaponSystem.cs:249, GameContext.cs:98). Deterministic nulls gain nothing from retry. Cited line numbers are off. Better fix: Keep the one-shot latch, but also retry when `_volume != null && _volume.Failed`. A read-back or worker failure (GunVolume.cs:631, :648, :680) leaves Ready false, and HandSolid never calls For again, so the proposed fix still leaves a permanent hold-long latch on that path. Gate the retry with a cap (about 3 tries) so a deterministic exception does not re-render a shape camera every second. Reload-assist paths wait 2-6 s on HandsSolid == null (ReloadInteractions.cs:1961, 2980, 3303), so the latch costs more than the finding says.
- [x] 251.17 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, minor, lead] src/Core/AutoTest.cs:1053: Flash check records 1.0 for guns it did not measure and never flags a gun that did not fire.
  What: Metric("lens flash check: share of shots that flashed ...", expected && shots > 0 ? (double)flashes / shots : 1.0); records 1.0 for guns with no muzzle flash by design and for guns with shots == 0. tooFew (line 1048) requires shots > 0, so a gun that did not fire adds no Problem; '(DID NOT FIRE)' appears only in the Results line (1051). Same sentinel-as-data pattern at AutoTest.cs:3104 (cases per shot records 0 when nothing fired) and 3330 (rounds per shot records 0 when fired == 0).
  Trigger: A weapon whose Fire never produces a shot (stuck, not unholstered, cooling down). FlashCheck passes it silently, and every flashless gun pulls the 'share of shots that flashed' average toward 1.0.
  Fix: Call Metric only when expected && shots > 0. When shots == 0 add Problem($"[{entry.name}] flash check: did not fire"). At 3104 and 3330, skip the Metric when nothing fired instead of recording 0.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code. AutoTest.cs:1053 records 1.0 for flashless guns (expected false) and for shots == 0, although the metric label says "(weapons with a muzzle flash)". tooFew (1048) needs shots > 0. FlashCheck mode quits at 340-346 and never reaches TestWeapons, whose DID NOT FIRE Problem is at 3118, so a non-firing gun adds no Problem. checkedGuns++ at 1045 is unconditional, so the 1064 summary says "guns fired" for guns that did not fire. 3104 and 3330 record 0 when nothing fired, as stated. Severity minor: it skews the averaged metric and hides a non-firing gun from the Problems count. Better fix: Call Metric only when expected && shots > 0. Add Problem($"[{entry.name}] flash check: did not fire") whenever shots == 0, not gated on expected, since a flashless gun that never fired is still unverified. Increment checkedGuns only when shots > 0 (or reword the 1064 summary to "guns tried"). At 3104 and 3330, skip the Metric call when nothing fired instead of recording 0.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed. AutoTest.cs:1053 records 1.0 for flashless guns (Any false, MuzzleFlashInfo.cs:24; Tasks 212 and LensFlash.cs:10 say such guns exist) and for shots==0, under a label saying only muzzle-flash weapons count. shots==0 is reachable: Weapon.cs:1040-1045 Fire refuses unless unholstered and CanFire, and CanFire (Weapon.cs:1406) fails for lock-on launchers without a target. FlashCheck adds no Problem for it (1048, 1058); "DID NOT FIRE" only reaches Results. Metric is report-only, and the flash check runs only with -rfvr-flashcheck. 3104 and 3330 are metric-only skews: non-fire already yields a Problem there (3118; ReportWeapon's Failure regex catches "NOT F"). Better fix: The Metric fix at 1053 is right. The proposed Problem for shots == 0 is incomplete: lock-on launchers with no target never fire by design, and AutoTest.cs:3108-3113 exempts them. Mirror that (targetTracker != null && !targetTracker.CanFire()) before adding "did not fire", or they get a false Problem. At 3330 no Problem is needed, since the line already flags through ReportWeapon. Only the Metric sentinel there should be dropped.
- [x] 251.18 (fixed: db57e7c) [confirmed, minor, helper] src/Core/EyeFilm.cs:156: Frame log written with the current culture; comma decimals break tools/flashfilm.py.
  What: Append(Time.unscaledTime.ToString("0.0000")) (EyeFilm.cs:156) and FilmState's $"flash {..:0.00} glow {..:0.00} worker {..:0.0} ms" (LensEffects.cs:124-125) use CurrentCulture. The mod already pins InvariantCulture for the same kind of number in VRConfig.cs:444 and 454, so the difference is an oversight. Nothing in src sets a culture globally.
  Trigger: Windows with a comma decimal separator (for example a German locale). frames.txt then holds '12,3456' and 'flash 0,50 glow 0,00 ...'. tools/flashfilm.py:41 float(p[2]) raises ValueError in read_film, reload_film does the same at line 176, and lens_num (line 53, regex ([0-9.]+)) reads '0,50' as 0.
  Fix: Use ToString("0.0000", CultureInfo.InvariantCulture) at EyeFilm.cs:156. In FilmState use string.Format(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, ...) or FormattableString.Invariant for the three numbers. Add using System.Globalization where needed.
  Skeptic (real): Verified. EyeFilm.cs:156 calls Time.unscaledTime.ToString("0.0000") with no CultureInfo, and LensEffects.cs:124-125 uses interpolated format specifiers, which are also culture-sensitive. Only VRConfig.cs:444 and :454 pass InvariantCulture, and nothing in src or tools sets a culture. flashfilm.py:41 and :176 call float() on the time field with no try, and main() (lines 192-200) has no guard, so a comma-decimal run crashes. Severity is minor: dev tooling only. The claimed lens_num zero is secondary, because the crash happens first. Better fix: The producer-side fix is right but incomplete. EyeFilm.cs and LensEffects.cs both lack "using System.Globalization;", so add it to both. Existing frames.txt logs already written with commas will still crash flashfilm.py, so also make the parser tolerant: float(p[2].replace(',', '.')) at flashfilm.py:41 and :176, and let the lens_num regex (line 53) and the worker regex (line 112) accept commas, then replace(',', '.') before float().
  Skeptic (real): Bug confirmed, reachable only in dev tooling. EyeFilm.cs:156 and LensEffects.cs:125 format with the current culture, and nothing in src sets a culture (only VRConfig.cs:444,454 pin Invariant). EyeFilm.Begin is called only from AutoTest.cs:760 and :914, both under FlashFilmRequested (-rfvr-flashfilm, AutoTest.cs:347). flashfilm.py:41 then raises ValueError on '12,3456', and lens_num (line 53) reads '0,50' as 0. Existing reports/shots/film frames.txt use dot decimals, so it has not fired yet. Minor. Better fix: Apply the proposed source fix: ToString("0.0000", CultureInfo.InvariantCulture) at EyeFilm.cs:156, and FormattableString.Invariant($"...") (or string.Format with InvariantCulture) for the FilmState string at LensEffects.cs:124-125, adding using System.Globalization where missing. Optionally make tools/flashfilm.py tolerant of comma logs (replace ',' with '.' before float() in read_film and reload_film, and in lens_num and the worker regex at line 112) so older logs still parse.
- [x] 251.19 (fixed: 3a79286) [confirmed, tiny, lead] src/Weapons/WeaponAnalyzer.cs:94: Cache key is only the GameObject name plus the entry name, so same-named weapons under one entry share an analysis.
  What: KeyOf: `string prefab = w.name.Replace("(Clone)", "").Trim(); entry = entry ?? w.weaponEntry; return entry != null ? entry.name + "#" + prefab : prefab;`. Nothing else identifies the Weapon component.
  Trigger: Two Weapon components under one entry with the same GameObject name, for example two under-barrel subs both named 'Weapon'. WeaponWarmup.cs:156 (`!WeaponAnalyzer.IsCached(sub, entry)`) skips the second. At draw, SubWeaponRig (HeldWeapon.cs:562) calls RigFor, which gets the first sub's data, so the second sub gets the wrong magazine and action. Subs analysed while weaponEntry is still null key on the bare name, so different entries can collide.
  Fix: Add the sub's path under its parent weapon (or its sibling index) to the key, and key sub-weapons under their parent entry.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. KeyOf (src/Weapons/WeaponAnalyzer.cs:94-99) keys on entry name plus GameObject name only. Subs take the parent's weaponEntry in Start (Weapon.cs:794, AddSubWeapon at Weapon.cs:2323), so same-named active subs under one entry share a key. WeaponWarmup.cs:156 skips the second, and SubWeaponRig (HeldWeapon.cs:563) gets the first sub's rig. The bare-name cross-entry collision is unreachable: SpawnWeapon sets weaponEntry at Actor.cs:830 before Start, and PlayerWeaponInVR excludes mounted weapons. Impact is narrow and needs duplicate sub names with different rigs, which the repo cannot show. Better fix: Drop the entry-keying part: subs already carry the parent's entry, so it changes nothing. Make the key include the sub's position under the root weapon: walk from w.transform up to the topmost Weapon ancestor, collect the sibling-index path, and key as entry.name + "#" + prefab + "/" + path (no suffix for the root). Prefab and clone then give the same key, so WeaponWarmup and the draw path agree. Keep the "#" format so the name regexes (Bren2Pattern, ArPattern) still match.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. KeyOf (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:94-99) keys on name only, and RigFor (WeaponSystem.cs:346-354) returns any cached entry for that key, so two same-named subs under one entry share a rig at draw time (HeldWeapon.cs:563). Duplicate sibling names are valid Unity content and nothing dedupes them, so the trigger can occur, though it is narrow. The bare-name clause is unreachable: SpawnWeapon sets the root's entry (Actor.cs:830) and AddSubWeapon copies it to subs at Start (Weapon.cs:794, 2323), before any sub can be equipped. Better fix: Drop the bare-name clause as a bug; it cannot happen on the live path. Key a sub by its child-index path from the top Weapon (climb transform.parent to the highest ancestor with a Weapon component; the path is identical on prefab and clone), appended to the existing "entry#prefab" key. Keep the "entry#" prefix because WeaponDrops.cs:726 matches on StartsWith(entry.name + "#"). A sub named exactly like its root also collides with the root under the current key; the path suffix fixes that too. Adjacent and larger: WeaponEntry.name defaults to "Weapon" (WeaponManager.cs:198), so unnamed entries from two mods with the same prefab name share a rig. Verify that case before relying on entry.name as identity.
- [-] (not a bug: the derivative and the spring share the clamped step) 251.20 [confirmed, tiny, helper] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:643: Inertia acceleration is differenced over the clamped frame time.
  What: `Vector3 raw = dt > 1e-4f ? (v - _handVelPrev) / dt : Vector3.zero;` (line 643) divides by `dt`, which BeforeRender set to `Mathf.Min(Time.unscaledDeltaTime, 0.05f)` (line 1042, passed to StepInertia at line 1099). `v` is the controller's measured velocity (VRHand.cs:135), sampled at real time. After a 0.1 s frame stall the velocity change covers 0.1 s but is divided by 0.05, so the acceleration fed to the lag spring is about doubled for that frame. The dead band and the 12/s smoothing (line 652) pass roughly half of that spike.
  Trigger: A frame hitch of 50 ms or more while the hand's velocity changes, for example a swing ending during a GC spike. The weight spring (`_lagPos`, `_lagPitch`) gets a jolt about twice the real acceleration for one frame. The effect is largest on a heavy launcher (omega 12), whose lag equilibrium is -A/omega^2.
  Fix: Difference over the real interval: pass `Time.unscaledDeltaTime` (guarded by 1e-4) to the derivative at line 643, and keep the clamped `dt` for the integration loop at lines 656-669.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code. HeldWeapon.cs:1059 sets dt = Mathf.Min(Time.unscaledDeltaTime, 0.05f) and passes it at :1116. StepInertia's only caller is :1116. Line 644 divides the velocity change by that clamped dt. VRRig.cs:767 calls VRHand.UpdatePose once per frame (VRHand.cs:135), so the samples are spaced by the real frame time. Hitches over 50 ms are therefore overestimated by unscaledDt/0.05: 2x at 0.1 s, 6x at 0.3 s, capped by the 40 m/s^2 clamp at :646. Real but small; the finding understates long hitches. Better fix: Apply the proposed fix, passing the unclamped value into StepInertia as a second argument (for example realDt = Time.unscaledDeltaTime). Line 644 then uses realDt with the 1e-4 guard. The smoothing at line 653 and the integration loop at lines 657-669 keep the clamped dt. The 1.2 m/s^2 dead band and the 40 m/s^2 clamp stay as they are.
  Skeptic (real): Mechanism confirmed. HeldWeapon.cs:644 divides (v - _handVelPrev) by dt, and BeforeRender:1059 clamps dt to 0.05 while the velocity is OpenVR's own vVelocity (VRInput.cs:511, used at VRHand.cs:130-135). It is sampled once per frame from VRRig.BeforeRender:767, so consecutive samples are one real frame apart. No stall reset exists: StepInertia (628-677) only resets on untracked poses. Reachable: AutoTest.cs:1570 notes hitches of most of a second. The error is real_dt/0.05, not a flat 2x, until the 40 m/s^2 clamp at line 646. The finding's line numbers are off by 1-17. Better fix: Keep the proposed fix, and note the error grows with the stall length rather than being a flat 2x. In StepInertia (or by passing it in), take float realDt = Time.unscaledDeltaTime and set raw = realDt > 1e-4f ? (v - _handVelPrev) / realDt : Vector3.zero; at line 644. Keep the clamped dt for the smoothing Lerp (line 653) and the integration loop (lines 657-670). The fix changes nothing on normal frames, where realDt equals dt.
- [x] 251.21 (fixed: d47af1d) [plausible, major, helper] src/Weapons/GunCollider.cs:614: Meshes longer than 1.5 m are treated as see-through and left out of the hand volume and the stand-in.
  What: SeeThrough ends with `|| r.bounds.size.sqrMagnitude > 1.5f * 1.5f;` (line 614). VisibleRenderers keeps only the non-see-through meshes in `solid` and returns that list whenever it is non-empty (lines 591 and 599). Its callers GunVolume.For (GunVolume.cs:286) and GunCollider.Fit (GunCollider.cs:528) therefore never see a long opaque mesh. The clause was added in commit d5a1423 for 'larger than a gun could be' (the 4 m holo quad). The additive, HUD and render-queue tests on the same expression already catch that quad.
  Trigger: A launcher whose tube is its own MeshRenderer longer than 1.5 m (Tasks.md item 199 records the AT-1K at 264 cm), with at least one other opaque mesh such as a grip or receiver. The tube is then absent from the hand solid and from the physics stand-in: the hand closes on air over the tube, and magazines and shells pass through it. A long single-mesh blade on a melee weapon is cut the same way, and MeasureBlade (ThrowAndMelee.cs:631) then reads a short blade length from the remaining pieces.
  Fix: Delete the size clause at GunCollider.cs:614. The envelope still bounds the volume (GunVolume.cs:431) and the fit (GunCollider.cs:541-546), so only a large opaque plane sitting inside that region would be kept. Check the holo sight in the autotest after the change.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed in code. SeeThrough (GunCollider.cs:604-615) is true when r.bounds.size.sqrMagnitude > 2.25, i.e. a world-AABB diagonal over 1.5 m, not a length (the expression is at line 615). VisibleRenderers (lines 589-600) drops see-through renderers from solid and returns solid whenever it is non-empty, so a long opaque tube beside a grip is missing from GunVolume.Build and Fit. The 264 cm AT-1K note (Tasks.md:341, Oct 14) predates the clause. Holo quad is additive per BoltPiece.cs:292. Better fix: Removing the clause is right for the holo quad (it is additive) but lets any large non-additive opaque plane inside the envelope into the hand volume and stand-in, since GunVolume.cs:431 clips only to the envelope. Safer: keep a guard above any real part, e.g. r.bounds.size.sqrMagnitude > 3.5f * 3.5f at GunCollider.cs:615 (a 4 m quad's AABB diagonal is about 5.7 m; a 2.64 m tube stays solid). Then check the holo in the autotest.
  Skeptic (not real): Mechanism exists: SeeThrough (GunCollider.cs:612-615) plus the solid filter (589-600) drop any opaque renderer with world AABB diagonal over 1.5 m. Trigger unsupported: the AT-1K 264 cm is MagBounds of the magazine/rocket transform (WeaponAnalyzer.cs:2111-2112), and MagazineParts are skipped before SeeThrough runs (GunCollider.cs:574-589). No in-repo evidence of a non-magazine opaque mesh over 1.5 m; non-zombie blades in autotest.txt read 26-76 cm. Better fix: Deleting the clause outright re-admits large opaque planes (the stray shadow plane the Envelope comment describes, GunCollider.cs:540) and also changes the projectile copy at MagazineProp.cs:685, which the finding does not mention. If the clause is kept, measure on local bounds scaled by lossyScale: r.bounds is pose-dependent, so a 1.0 x 0.5 x 0.5 m opaque box has a 1.5 m diagonal axis-aligned but 2.5 m at 45 degrees. The 4 m holo quad is additive (commit d5a1423 message), so the shader tests already cover it.
- [-] (comment only: an unseated magazine falls, as in play) 251.22 [plausible, tiny, lead] src/Weapons/ReloadInteractions.cs:2433: A magazine let go unseated is dropped before the 'back into the pouch' branch.

Drop the reserve-loss claim, since rounds stay on the prop and return on despawn. Whether the well sits inside the 17 cm pouch radius (BodyZones.cs:33, 418) is not verifiable statically.

Every ReleaseProp path clears off.Held (Drop, MagazineProp.cs:1882-1888), so 2441-2447 is dead code. The rounds are not lost: the floor magazine stays pickable, and Despawn returns its rounds to the reserve (MagazineProp.cs:1963; ReturnRoundsOnMagDrop defaults true, VRConfig.cs:215) after 60 s. The pouch-zone claim for the let-go point is unverified. Better fix: No functional fix is needed. Delete the unreachable branch at ReloadInteractions.cs:2441-2447 and its stale comment, or leave it. Do not move the unseated magazine into the pouch at 2433: the floor drop is the normal let-go behaviour, and the rounds already return to the reserve on despawn. Changing it would alter the film for no gameplay gain.
- [-] (not a bug: as 251.20) 251.23 [plausible, tiny, helper] src/Weapons/HeldWeapon.cs:643: Weight-lag acceleration divides by a clamped frame time on stalls.
  What: BeforeRender sets `float dt = Mathf.Min(Time.unscaledDeltaTime, 0.05f)` (1042) and passes it to StepInertia, which takes the acceleration as `(v - _handVelPrev) / dt` (643). v is main.BodyRelativeVelocity, sampled once per real frame in VRHand.UpdatePose (VRRig.cs:767), so after a frame stall the velocity change spans the real, longer interval but is divided by 0.05.
  Trigger: A 150 ms hitch while the hand changes speed. The derivative reads about 3 times the true acceleration for that frame, so after the 1.2 m/s^2 dead band and the 40 m/s^2 clamp the lag spring gets a kick larger than the hand's real motion gives.
  Fix: Divide by Time.unscaledDeltaTime (unclamped) for the derivative, and keep the 0.05 clamp only for the spring integration steps (656-669).
  Skeptic (not real): The numbers are right: HeldWeapon.cs:1042 clamps dt to 0.05 and line 643 divides by it, so a 150 ms frame reads 3x the true acceleration. But the same clamped dt drives the spring (656-669, called at 1099), so the frame delivers only 0.05 s of forcing. raw*dt equals the hand's real velocity change, so the total lag impulse matches the real motion rather than exceeding it. The velocity sample is taken once per render frame (VRRig.cs:767, VRHand.cs:135), so the interval is consistent. Better fix: Do not apply the proposed fix. Dividing by unclamped dt while integrating over the clamped 0.05 s would cut the stall-frame lag impulse to about a third of the hand's real velocity change (0.05a instead of 0.15a). The current shared clamp keeps the impulse consistent. The only residue is that the spring sees that impulse in 50 ms instead of 150 ms, a time-base choice; if that should follow real time, change the derivative and the spring together on one time base (for example real dt capped at 0.1 s with more substeps) and judge it on hardware.
  Skeptic (real): Confirmed, severity tiny. VRRig.BeforeRender calls VRHand.UpdatePose (VRRig.cs:767) before HeldWeapon reads BodyRelativeVelocity, and both run from OnBeforeRender once per frame (VRManager.cs:228-235). The velocity is the runtime's vVelocity (VRInput.cs:510), not dt-scaled, so consecutive samples span the real gap. HeldWeapon.cs:1042 clamps dt to 0.05 and line 643 divides by it. Nothing resets on stalls (629-633). Trigger: any frame over 50 ms with a hand speed change. The error grows with stall length, capped by the 40 m/s^2 clamp. Better fix: The proposed fix is right and changes nothing below 20 fps. Keep the raw dt at 1042 (float rawDt = Time.unscaledDeltaTime; dt = Mathf.Min(rawDt, 0.05f)), pass rawDt into StepInertia, and use it for the derivative at 643 and its 1e-4 guard. Keep the clamped dt for the lerp at 652 and the spring steps 656-669. Optional refinement: take the exact sample gap from VRHand._timeHistory (VRHand.cs:140) in case a rendered frame is ever skipped.

MatchShape counts beyond-box interior as excess (GunCollider.cs:456, GunVolume.cs:250), so for the stated overhang the cut hull usually wins and hands and props agree.
